`README.md`:

```md
# IDA-Plugins

A collection of IDA Pro / Hex-Rays plugins, built and installed together.

## Plugins

| Directory | Target | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `vtable-utility/` | `pseudocode_xrefs` | Virtual-call recognition, inheritance-aware VTable navigation, renaming, prototypes, and cross-references. |
| `stl-utility/` | `stl_retype` | One-click retyping and SSO-aware content preview for MSVC STL containers (`std::string`, `shared_ptr`, `vector`, `unique_ptr`, header-only `map`/`set`/`unordered_map`/`unordered_set` stats), plus collapsing inlined `shared_ptr`/`string`/`std::function`/`vector` (trivial-element only) release and closure-teardown boilerplate. |
| `xxhash-utility/` | `xxhash_collapse` | Recognizes compiler-inlined `XXH32`/`XXH64` and collapses them into a readable call. |
| `sm-global-finder/` | `sm_global_finder` | Discovers MSVC RTTI singleton construction/allocation chains and renames the constructor, allocator, and backing global; also walks the real MSVC RTTI structures (Complete Object Locator / Class Hierarchy Descriptor) to recover each class's base classes and annotates its vtable with an inheritance comment; also synthesizes a best-effort struct layout for each class from its constructor's field-initialization writes, registered into Local Types for manual review (not auto-applied, to avoid silently corrupting decompilation with a wrong guess). |
| `crt-idioms-utility/` | `crt_idioms_collapse` | Recognizes compiler-inlined CRT idioms (currently: inlined `strlen` loops) and collapses them into a readable call. |
| `sm-lua-func-finder/` | `sm_lua_func_finder` | Discovers Lua-callable C functions registered via `luaL_register()` (Scrap Mechanic's `sm.*` static namespaces and userdata methods) and renames them `Lua_<Owner>_<func>`, adding doc comments from the `sm_api_differ` json dump where available; also collapses the `lua_gettop`/`luaL_error` argument-count-check boilerplate that opens nearly every one of them into a single `L_checkArgCount(L, N)` call. |

Each plugin is a self-contained CMake project (its own `CMakeLists.txt` /
`ida-plugin.json`), sharing the same build conventions.

## Requirements

- IDA Professional 9.2 or newer, Hex-Rays decompiler
- IDA SDK configured through the `IDASDK` environment variable (a checkout of
  [HexRaysSA/ida-sdk](https://github.com/HexRaysSA/ida-sdk) at a tag with
  CMake support, e.g. `v9.3.1-release` -- not the classic hex-rays SDK zip)
- CMake 3.27+, Visual Studio with C++ build tools

## Install everything

```powershell
$env:IDASDK = "C:\path\to\ida-sdk"
.\install.ps1
```

This builds every plugin (Release, x64) and copies each one into
`<IDA install dir>\plugins\<target>\`. Since that's usually under
`Program Files`, the script re-launches itself elevated (a UAC prompt) if
it isn't already running as Administrator.

To build/install a single plugin manually instead, see that plugin's own
README.

## Diagnostic logs

Each plugin writes its own log to `%APPDATA%\Hex-Rays\IDA Pro\<name>.log`.

```

`crt-idioms-utility/CMakeLists.txt`:

```txt
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.27)

project(IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility LANGUAGES CXX)

set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 17)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)
set(CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS OFF)

if(NOT DEFINED ENV{IDASDK} OR "$ENV{IDASDK}" STREQUAL "")
  message(FATAL_ERROR "Set IDASDK to the IDA SDK root before configuring.")
endif()

include("$ENV{IDASDK}/src/cmake/bootstrap.cmake")
find_package(idasdk REQUIRED)

ida_add_plugin(crt_idioms_collapse
  SOURCES
    src/crt_idioms_plugin.cpp
    src/strlen_match.cpp
  METADATA_JSON
    ida-plugin.json
)

if(WIN32)
  set(IDA_INSTALL_DIR "" CACHE PATH "IDA installation directory for plugin deployment")
  set(IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_DIR "${IDA_INSTALL_DIR}")
  if("${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_DIR}" STREQUAL "")
    file(GLOB IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_CANDIDATES
      LIST_DIRECTORIES true
      "C:/Program Files/IDA Professional *"
      "C:/Program Files/IDA Pro *"
      "C:/Program Files/IDA *")
    list(SORT IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_CANDIDATES
      COMPARE NATURAL
      ORDER DESCENDING)
    list(LENGTH IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_CANDIDATES IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_CANDIDATE_COUNT)
    if(IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_CANDIDATE_COUNT GREATER 0)
      list(GET IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_CANDIDATES 0 IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_DIR)
    endif()
  endif()

  if(NOT "${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_DIR}" STREQUAL "")
    set(IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_PLUGIN_DIR
      "${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_INSTALL_DIR}/plugins/crt_idioms_collapse")

    add_custom_command(TARGET crt_idioms_collapse POST_BUILD
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E make_directory
        "${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_PLUGIN_DIR}"
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E copy_if_different
        "$<TARGET_FILE:crt_idioms_collapse>"
        "${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_PLUGIN_DIR}/$<TARGET_FILE_NAME:crt_idioms_collapse>"
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E copy_if_different
        "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/ida-plugin.json"
        "${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_PLUGIN_DIR}/ida-plugin.json"
      COMMENT "Installing IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility in ${IDA_CRT_IDIOMS_UTILITY_PLUGIN_DIR}"
    )
  else()
    message(WARNING "No IDA installation found; skipping post-build plugin deployment.")
  endif()
endif()

```

`crt-idioms-utility/README.md`:

```md
# IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility

Recognizes compiler-inlined CRT idioms in Hex-Rays pseudocode and collapses
them into a single readable call, so you see what the code is doing instead
of the compiler's inlined implementation.

### NOTE: This project is fully AI generated, I don't take any credit for anything in here.

## Supported idioms (v1)

- Inlined `strlen`: MSVC frequently inlines `strlen` as

  ```c
  counter = -1;
  do
    ++counter;
  while ( *(_BYTE *)(ptr + counter) );
  ```

  This is collapsed into:

  ```c
  counter = strlen(ptr);
  ```

## Usage

No user interaction required -- the collapse pass runs automatically on
every decompile, right before pseudocode text is generated (at
`hxe_maturity`/`CMAT_FINAL`).

## Requirements

- IDA Professional 9.2 or newer
- Hex-Rays decompiler
- IDA SDK configured through the `IDASDK` environment variable (a checkout of
  [HexRaysSA/ida-sdk](https://github.com/HexRaysSA/ida-sdk) at a tag with
  CMake support, e.g. `v9.3.1-release` -- not the classic hex-rays SDK zip)
- CMake 3.27 or newer
- Visual Studio with C++ build tools on Windows

## Build

```powershell
$env:IDASDK = "C:\path\to\ida-sdk"
cmake -S . -B build -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64
cmake --build build --config Release
```

On Windows, the build installs the DLL and metadata into IDA's plugin
directory automatically if it has write access; otherwise copy
`crt_idioms_collapse.dll` and `ida-plugin.json` into
`<IDA install>\plugins\crt_idioms_collapse\` yourself (this typically needs
an elevated copy, since `Program Files` is admin-writable only).

## Diagnostic output

Diagnostic output is written to:

```text
%APPDATA%\Hex-Rays\IDA Pro\IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility.log
```

## Known limitations (v1)

- Only recognizes the do-while-with-counter-initialized-to--1 shape of
  inlined `strlen` shown above; other inlining shapes (e.g. loop-unrolled or
  SIMD-vectorized variants) are not recognized.
- Does not verify that `ptr` is actually a `char*`/byte pointer beyond the
  byte-sized dereference in the loop condition -- unrelated do-while loops
  that happen to match this exact shape (rare) would also be collapsed.

```

`crt-idioms-utility/src/crt_idioms_plugin.cpp`:

```cpp
// IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility: recognizes compiler-inlined CRT idioms (currently:
// inlined `strlen` loops) and collapses them into a readable call. Mirrors
// the build/plugin conventions of the sister IDA-STL-Utility plugin.

#include "strlen_match.h"

#include <hexrays.hpp>
#include <kernwin.hpp>
#include <diskio.hpp>
#include <fpro.h>

#include <cstdarg>

namespace
{

using namespace crt_idioms;

// Writes to %APPDATA%\Hex-Rays\IDA Pro\IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility.log, mirroring
// the sister plugins' debug_log convention.
void debug_log(const char *format, ...)
{
  qstring path;
  path.sprnt("%s/IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility.log", get_user_idadir());
  FILE *file = qfopen(path.c_str(), "a");
  if ( file == nullptr )
    return;

  qstring message;
  va_list args;
  va_start(args, format);
  message.vsprnt(format, args);
  va_end(args);
  qfprintf(
    file,
    "[%u.%06u] %s\n",
    get_secs(qtime64()),
    get_usecs(qtime64()),
    message.c_str());
  qfclose(file);
}

ssize_t idaapi decompiler_callback(void *, hexrays_event_t event, va_list va)
{
  if ( event == hxe_maturity )
  {
    cfunc_t *cfunc = va_arg(va, cfunc_t *);
    const ctree_maturity_t new_maturity = ctree_maturity_t(va_arg(va, int));
    if ( new_maturity == CMAT_FINAL )
    {
      const int collapsed = collapse_strlen_idioms(cfunc);
      if ( collapsed > 0 )
        debug_log("collapsed %d inlined strlen idiom(s) in function=%a", collapsed, cfunc->entry_ea);
    }
    return 0;
  }

  return 0;
}

struct crt_idioms_plugmod_t final : plugmod_t
{
  crt_idioms_plugmod_t()
  {
    if ( !install_hexrays_callback(decompiler_callback, this) )
      msg("%s: failed to install the Hex-Rays callback\n", PLUGIN.wanted_name);

    debug_log("plugin initialized: version=0.1.0");
  }

  ~crt_idioms_plugmod_t() override
  {
    remove_hexrays_callback(decompiler_callback, this);
  }

  bool idaapi run(size_t) override
  {
    return false;
  }
};

plugmod_t *idaapi init()
{
  if ( !init_hexrays_plugin() )
    return nullptr;
  return new crt_idioms_plugmod_t;
}

} // namespace

plugin_t PLUGIN =
{
  IDP_INTERFACE_VERSION,
  PLUGIN_HIDE | PLUGIN_MULTI,
  init,
  nullptr,
  nullptr,
  "Recognizes compiler-inlined CRT idioms (currently: inlined strlen loops) and collapses them into a readable call.",
  "",
  "IDA-CRT-Idioms-Utility",
  ""
};

```

`crt-idioms-utility/src/strlen_match.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "strlen_match.h"

#include <typeinf.hpp>

#include <set>

namespace crt_idioms
{

namespace
{

const cexpr_t *strip_casts(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  while ( e != nullptr && e->op == cot_cast )
    e = e->x;
  return e;
}

bool is_num(const cexpr_t *e, int64 value)
{
  return e != nullptr && e->op == cot_num && int64(e->numval()) == value;
}

bool is_var(const cexpr_t *e, int var_idx)
{
  return e != nullptr && e->op == cot_var && e->v.idx == var_idx;
}

// If `body` is a cit_block with exactly one statement, returns that
// statement; if `body` itself isn't a block, returns it directly (a
// single, brace-less statement); otherwise (empty/multi-statement block)
// returns nullptr.
cinsn_t *single_body_stmt(cinsn_t *body)
{
  if ( body == nullptr )
    return nullptr;
  if ( body->op != cit_block )
    return body;
  if ( body->cblock == nullptr || body->cblock->size() != 1 )
    return nullptr;
  return &body->cblock->front();
}

// True if `stmt` (a cit_expr, possibly wrapping the whole statement) is one
// of:
//   ++counter;   / counter++;   (cot_preinc/cot_postinc on counter)
//   counter += 1;               (cot_asgadd, since Hex-Rays sometimes prints
//                                 "x += 1" as a distinct opcode rather than
//                                 folding it into cot_preinc/cot_postinc --
//                                 lesson learned in IDA-XXHash-Utility)
bool match_increment(cinsn_t *stmt, int counter_idx)
{
  if ( stmt == nullptr || stmt->op != cit_expr || stmt->cexpr == nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *e = stmt->cexpr;
  if ( e->op == cot_preinc || e->op == cot_postinc )
    return is_var(strip_casts(e->x), counter_idx);

  if ( e->op == cot_asgadd )
    return is_var(strip_casts(e->x), counter_idx) && is_num(strip_casts(e->y), 1);

  return false;
}

// If `binop` is `PTR + counter` or `counter + PTR` (a cot_add/cot_idx whose
// operands are, in either order, a pointer expression and the counter
// var), returns the pointer-expression operand; otherwise nullptr.
const cexpr_t *match_ptr_plus_counter(const cexpr_t *binop, int counter_idx)
{
  if ( binop == nullptr || binop->x == nullptr || binop->y == nullptr )
    return nullptr;
  if ( is_var(strip_casts(binop->x), counter_idx) )
    return binop->y;
  if ( is_var(strip_casts(binop->y), counter_idx) )
    return binop->x;
  return nullptr;
}

// True if `cond` (the do-while's trailing condition) is a truthiness test
// on `*(_BYTE *)(ptr + counter)`, tolerating both the bare deref (used
// directly as a truthy condition) and an explicit "!= 0" comparison. On
// success, returns the recovered pointer sub-expression (still owned by
// the ctree -- caller must clone before reusing it elsewhere).
const cexpr_t *match_byte_deref_condition(const cexpr_t *cond, int counter_idx)
{
  const cexpr_t *e = strip_casts(cond);
  if ( e == nullptr )
    return nullptr;

  if ( e->op == cot_ne )
  {
    const cexpr_t *a = strip_casts(e->x);
    const cexpr_t *b = strip_casts(e->y);
    const cexpr_t *deref = nullptr;
    if ( is_num(a, 0) )
      deref = e->y;
    else if ( is_num(b, 0) )
      deref = e->x;
    if ( deref == nullptr )
      return nullptr;
    e = strip_casts(deref);
  }
  if ( e == nullptr )
    return nullptr;

  if ( e->op == cot_ptr )
  {
    const cexpr_t *inner = strip_casts(e->x);
    if ( inner == nullptr || inner->op != cot_add )
      return nullptr;
    return match_ptr_plus_counter(inner, counter_idx);
  }
  if ( e->op == cot_idx )
    return match_ptr_plus_counter(e, counter_idx);

  return nullptr;
}

struct strlen_match_t
{
  cinsn_t *asg_stmt = nullptr;      // "counter = -1;"
  cinsn_t *do_stmt = nullptr;       // the sibling do-while right after it
  const cexpr_t *counter_var = nullptr; // the cot_var LHS of asg_stmt (for cloning)
  const cexpr_t *ptr_expr = nullptr;    // recovered string pointer (for cloning)
};

// Matches the "counter = -1;" / "do ++counter; while (*(ptr+counter));"
// pair of adjacent statements. `asg_stmt` and `do_stmt` must already be
// known to be adjacent siblings within the same cblock_t.
bool try_match_pair(cinsn_t *asg_stmt, cinsn_t *do_stmt, strlen_match_t *out)
{
  if ( asg_stmt == nullptr || asg_stmt->op != cit_expr || asg_stmt->cexpr == nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *asg = asg_stmt->cexpr;
  if ( asg->op != cot_asg )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *lhs = strip_casts(asg->x);
  if ( lhs == nullptr || lhs->op != cot_var )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *rhs = strip_casts(asg->y);
  if ( rhs == nullptr || rhs->op != cot_num || int64(rhs->numval()) != -1 )
    return false;

  const int counter_idx = lhs->v.idx;

  if ( do_stmt == nullptr || do_stmt->op != cit_do || do_stmt->cdo == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *inc_stmt = single_body_stmt(do_stmt->cdo->body);
  if ( !match_increment(inc_stmt, counter_idx) )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *ptr_expr = match_byte_deref_condition(&do_stmt->cdo->expr, counter_idx);
  if ( ptr_expr == nullptr )
    return false;

  out->asg_stmt = asg_stmt;
  out->do_stmt = do_stmt;
  out->counter_var = lhs;
  out->ptr_expr = ptr_expr;
  return true;
}

bool synthesize_and_replace(const strlen_match_t &match)
{
  if ( match.asg_stmt == nullptr || match.counter_var == nullptr || match.ptr_expr == nullptr )
    return false;

  tinfo_t int_type;
  if ( !int_type.create_simple_type(BTF_INT32) )
    return false;

  carglist_t *args = new carglist_t();
  args->resize(1);
  args->at(0).consume_cexpr(new cexpr_t(*match.ptr_expr));

  cexpr_t *call = call_helper(int_type, args, "strlen");
  if ( call == nullptr )
    return false;

  cexpr_t *counter_clone = new cexpr_t(*match.counter_var);

  cexpr_t *assign = new cexpr_t();
  assign->op = cot_asg;
  assign->x = counter_clone;
  assign->y = call;
  assign->type = counter_clone->type;
  assign->exflags = 0;

  cinsn_t *new_stmt = new cinsn_t();
  new_stmt->op = cit_expr;
  new_stmt->cexpr = assign;
  new_stmt->ea = match.asg_stmt->ea;

  match.asg_stmt->replace_by(new_stmt);
  return true;
}

struct strlen_collapser_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  int collapsed_count = 0;

  strlen_collapser_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  int idaapi visit_insn(cinsn_t *insn) override
  {
    if ( insn->op != cit_block || insn->cblock == nullptr )
      return 0;

    cblock_t *block = insn->cblock;
    for ( cblock_t::iterator it = block->begin(); it != block->end(); )
    {
      cblock_t::iterator next_it = it;
      ++next_it;
      if ( next_it == block->end() )
        break;

      strlen_match_t match;
      if ( try_match_pair(&*it, &*next_it, &match) && synthesize_and_replace(match) )
      {
        ++collapsed_count;
        // The assignment statement (`it`) was mutated in-place via
        // replace_by(); the do-while sibling (`next_it`) is now dead
        // weight and must be erased from the block, not left behind.
        it = block->erase(next_it);
        continue;
      }

      ++it;
    }
    return 0;
  }
};

struct used_var_collector_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  std::set<int> used_indices;

  used_var_collector_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op == cot_var )
      used_indices.insert(e->v.idx);
    return 0;
  }
};

// See IDA-STL-Utility's stl_shared_ptr_release.cpp for the rationale:
// replace_by/erase deletes statements but leaves now-orphaned locals (the
// loop counter, cached pointer, etc.) declared-but-unused, since their
// CVAR_USED flag isn't recalculated after our mutation. Hide anything no
// longer referenced, skipping arguments (never hide those -- it would
// alter the printed function signature, not just a declaration line).
void hide_orphaned_locals(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  used_var_collector_t collector;
  collector.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);

  lvars_t *lvars = cfunc->get_lvars();
  if ( lvars == nullptr )
    return;

  for ( size_t i = 0; i < lvars->size(); ++i )
  {
    lvar_t &lvar = lvars->at(i);
    if ( lvar.is_arg_var() || !lvar.used() )
      continue;
    if ( collector.used_indices.find(int(i)) == collector.used_indices.end() )
      lvar.clear_used();
  }
}

} // namespace

int collapse_strlen_idioms(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  strlen_collapser_t collapser;
  collapser.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);
  if ( collapser.collapsed_count > 0 )
    hide_orphaned_locals(cfunc);
  return collapser.collapsed_count;
}

} // namespace crt_idioms

```

`crt-idioms-utility/src/strlen_match.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <hexrays.hpp>

namespace crt_idioms
{

// Recognizes the inlined MSVC strlen idiom:
//   counter = -1;
//   do
//     ++counter;
//   while ( *(_BYTE *)(ptr + counter) );
// This is `strlen` fully inlined by the compiler: a counter initialized to
// -1, incremented in a do-while whose trailing condition tests the byte at
// (ptr + counter) for non-zero. Once the loop exits, `counter` holds the
// string length (not counting the terminator). This is pure CRT boilerplate
// -- not useful to a reverse engineer -- so it's collapsed into a single
// `counter = strlen((char*)ptr);`-shaped synthetic call, mirroring the
// sister IDA-STL-Utility / IDA-XXHash-Utility plugins' approach to
// collapsing inlined boilerplate. Runs at hxe_maturity/CMAT_FINAL, mutating
// the ctree via cinsn_t::replace_by before pseudocode text is generated.
//
// Returns the number of collapsed occurrences within `cfunc`'s body.
int collapse_strlen_idioms(cfunc_t *cfunc);

} // namespace crt_idioms

```

`install.ps1`:

```ps1
<#
.SYNOPSIS
  Builds every plugin in this repo and installs it into the local IDA
  Professional installation's plugins directory.

.DESCRIPTION
  Configures + builds each plugin with CMake (Release, x64), then copies
  the built DLL and ida-plugin.json into
  "<IDA install dir>\plugins\<target>\". Copying into the IDA install
  directory (under Program Files) requires administrator rights, so this
  script re-launches itself elevated (a UAC prompt) if it isn't already
  running as Administrator -- the whole build+install runs in that
  elevated process.

.PARAMETER IdaSdk
  Path to a HexRaysSA/ida-sdk checkout (a tag with CMake support, e.g.
  v9.3.1-release -- not the classic hex-rays SDK zip). Defaults to the
  IDASDK environment variable, or C:\Users\Ben\ida-sdk-src if that's unset.

.PARAMETER IdaInstallDir
  IDA installation directory to install into. If omitted, the newest
  "C:\Program Files\IDA Professional *" / "IDA Pro *" / "IDA *" directory
  is auto-detected, matching each plugin's own CMakeLists.txt logic.

.PARAMETER Only
  Directory name(s) of specific plugin(s) to build/install (e.g.
  "xxhash-utility"), instead of all of them. Useful when other plugins
  have in-progress edits you don't want to rebuild yet.
#>

[CmdletBinding()]
param(
  [string]$IdaSdk = $(if ($env:IDASDK) { $env:IDASDK } else { "C:\Users\Ben\ida-sdk-src" }),
  [string]$IdaInstallDir,
  # Comma-separated list (e.g. "stl-utility,xxhash-utility"), not string[] --
  # when this script re-launches itself elevated via `-File`, PowerShell
  # does NOT apply its interactive "a,b splits into an array" parsing to
  # passed-through arguments (that only happens for arguments typed directly
  # in a PowerShell expression, not raw argv passed via -File), so a
  # string[] parameter arrives as a single unsplit string anyway -- a plain
  # string split manually below avoids that mismatch entirely.
  [string]$Only
)

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

# --- Self-elevate (UAC prompt) if not already running as Administrator ---
$currentPrincipal = New-Object Security.Principal.WindowsPrincipal(
  [Security.Principal.WindowsIdentity]::GetCurrent())
if (-not $currentPrincipal.IsInRole([Security.Principal.WindowsBuiltinRole]::Administrator)) {
  Write-Host "Elevation required to install into Program Files -- relaunching with UAC prompt..."
  $scriptArgs = @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-File", $PSCommandPath)
  foreach ($key in $PSBoundParameters.Keys) {
    $value = $PSBoundParameters[$key]
    $scriptArgs += "-$key"
    if ($value -is [array]) {
      # Comma-join (not space-join, which is PowerShell's default array
      # ToString() and gets mis-split back into separate positional tokens
      # by the child process's command-line parsing) so string[] params
      # round-trip correctly through the elevated relaunch.
      $scriptArgs += ($value -join ",")
    } else {
      $scriptArgs += "$value"
    }
  }
  Start-Process powershell -Verb RunAs -ArgumentList $scriptArgs
  exit
}

$repoRoot = $PSScriptRoot

# --- Log everything to a file so results are inspectable even if this
#     elevated window closes/crashes before anyone reads it interactively. ---
$logPath = Join-Path $repoRoot "install.log"
$transcribing = $false
try {
  Start-Transcript -Path $logPath -Append -ErrorAction Stop | Out-Null
  $transcribing = $true
} catch {
  Write-Warning "Could not start transcript logging to $logPath -- continuing without it: $_"
}

try {
  Write-Host ""
  Write-Host "===== install.ps1 run started: $(Get-Date -Format o) =====" -ForegroundColor DarkGray

  if (-not (Test-Path (Join-Path $IdaSdk "src\cmake\bootstrap.cmake"))) {
    throw "IDASDK ('$IdaSdk') doesn't look like a CMake-enabled ida-sdk checkout (missing src\cmake\bootstrap.cmake). See each plugin's README for setup."
  }
  $env:IDASDK = $IdaSdk
  Write-Host "Using IDASDK: $IdaSdk"

  if (-not $IdaInstallDir) {
    $candidates = Get-ChildItem "C:\Program Files" -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
      Where-Object { $_.Name -like "IDA Professional *" -or $_.Name -like "IDA Pro *" -or $_.Name -like "IDA *" } |
      Sort-Object Name -Descending
    if (-not $candidates) {
      throw "No IDA installation found under C:\Program Files. Pass -IdaInstallDir explicitly."
    }
    $IdaInstallDir = $candidates[0].FullName
  }
  Write-Host "Installing into: $IdaInstallDir"

  $plugins = @(
    @{ Dir = "vtable-utility";   Target = "pseudocode_xrefs" }
    @{ Dir = "stl-utility";      Target = "stl_retype" }
    @{ Dir = "xxhash-utility";   Target = "xxhash_collapse" }
    @{ Dir = "sm-global-finder"; Target = "sm_global_finder" }
    @{ Dir = "crt-idioms-utility"; Target = "crt_idioms_collapse" }
    @{ Dir = "sm-lua-func-finder"; Target = "sm_lua_func_finder" }
  )

  if ($Only) {
    $onlyList = $Only.Split(",") | ForEach-Object { $_.Trim() } | Where-Object { $_ }
    $plugins = $plugins | Where-Object { $onlyList -contains $_.Dir }
    if (-not $plugins) {
      throw "No plugins matched -Only $($onlyList -join ', ')"
    }
  }

  $results = @()

  foreach ($plugin in $plugins) {
    $pluginDir = Join-Path $repoRoot $plugin.Dir
    $buildDir = Join-Path $pluginDir "build"
    $target = $plugin.Target

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== $($plugin.Dir) ($target) ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    if (-not (Test-Path $pluginDir)) {
      Write-Warning "Skipping $($plugin.Dir): directory not found."
      $results += [pscustomobject]@{ Plugin = $plugin.Dir; Status = "missing directory" }
      continue
    }

    try {
      & cmake -S $pluginDir -B $buildDir -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64
      if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "cmake configure failed (exit $LASTEXITCODE)" }

      & cmake --build $buildDir --config Release
      if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "cmake build failed (exit $LASTEXITCODE)" }

      $builtDll = Join-Path $IdaSdk "src\bin\plugins\$target\$target.dll"
      $builtJson = Join-Path $IdaSdk "src\bin\plugins\$target\ida-plugin.json"
      if (-not (Test-Path $builtDll)) {
        throw "Built DLL not found at expected path: $builtDll"
      }

      $destDir = Join-Path $IdaInstallDir "plugins\$target"
      New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $destDir | Out-Null
      Copy-Item $builtDll $destDir -Force
      if (Test-Path $builtJson) {
        Copy-Item $builtJson $destDir -Force
      }

      Write-Host "Installed $target -> $destDir" -ForegroundColor Green
      $results += [pscustomobject]@{ Plugin = $plugin.Dir; Status = "installed" }
    }
    catch {
      Write-Warning "Failed to build/install $($plugin.Dir): $_"
      $results += [pscustomobject]@{ Plugin = $plugin.Dir; Status = "FAILED: $_" }
    }
  }

  Write-Host ""
  Write-Host "=== Summary ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
  $results | Format-Table -AutoSize | Out-String | Write-Host

  Write-Host ""
  Write-Host "Restart IDA (if running) to pick up the installed/updated plugins."
  Write-Host "===== install.ps1 run finished: $(Get-Date -Format o) =====" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
}
catch {
  # Catch-all so a top-level/unexpected exception still gets written to the
  # log before the window (and transcript) closes, instead of vanishing.
  Write-Host ""
  Write-Host "===== UNHANDLED ERROR =====" -ForegroundColor Red
  Write-Host "$_" -ForegroundColor Red
  Write-Host ($_.ScriptStackTrace) -ForegroundColor Red
  Write-Host "===== install.ps1 run FAILED: $(Get-Date -Format o) =====" -ForegroundColor DarkGray
}
finally {
  if ($transcribing) {
    try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch {}
  }
}

Read-Host "Press Enter to close"

```

  message(FATAL_ERROR "Set IDASDK to the IDA SDK root before configuring.")

  SOURCES
    src/rtti_scan.cpp

    src/struct_synth.cpp
    src/plugin_main.cpp

if(WIN32)
  set(IDA_INSTALL_DIR "" CACHE PATH "IDA installation directory for plugin deployment")
  set(SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR "${IDA_INSTALL_DIR}")
  if("${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR}" STREQUAL "")
    file(GLOB SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES
      LIST_DIRECTORIES true
      "C:/Program Files/IDA Professional *"
      "C:/Program Files/IDA Pro *"
      "C:/Program Files/IDA *")
    list(SORT SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES
      COMPARE NATURAL
      ORDER DESCENDING)
    list(LENGTH SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATE_COUNT)
    if(SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATE_COUNT GREATER 0)
      list(GET SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES 0 SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR)
    endif()
  endif()

  if(NOT "${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR}" STREQUAL "")
    set(SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR
      "${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR}/plugins/sm_global_finder")

    add_custom_command(TARGET sm_global_finder POST_BUILD
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E make_directory
        "${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}"
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E copy_if_different
        "$<TARGET_FILE:sm_global_finder>"
        "${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}/$<TARGET_FILE_NAME:sm_global_finder>"
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E copy_if_different
        "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/ida-plugin.json"
        "${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}/ida-plugin.json"
      COMMENT "Installing SM Global Finder in ${SM_GLOBAL_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}"
    )
  else()
    message(WARNING "No IDA installation found; skipping post-build plugin deployment.")
  endif()
endif()

`sm-global-finder/README.md`:

```md
# sm-global-finder

An IDA Pro / Hex-Rays plugin (`sm_global_finder`) that mines MSVC RTTI data
already present in the binary to recover information about singletons and
class hierarchies, purely from static data structures -- no decompilation
required for either feature.

## Features

### Singleton chain discovery

For every MSVC RTTI vtable found (`??_7<ClassName>@@6B@` symbols), the plugin
walks the vft -> constructor -> allocator -> global chain: it finds the
function(s) that stamp the vtable pointer into an object, follows their
callers looking for an `operator new` + construct wrapper (or a
self-registering constructor that stores its own `this` into a global), and
renames the discovered constructor, allocator (as `ClassName::create`, since
it unconditionally allocates and constructs a fresh instance on every call --
it is a factory, not a lazy singleton accessor with a cached-instance check),
and backing global (as `g_ClassName`). A comment summarizing the chain is
added to the constructor and allocator functions.

### RTTI class hierarchy recovery

Independently of the singleton scan, the plugin also walks the actual MSVC
RTTI data structures for every discovered vtable -- the
`_RTTICompleteObjectLocator` pointer stored at `vft_ea - 8`, its
`_RTTIClassHierarchyDescriptor`, and its base-class array of
`_RTTIBaseClassDescriptor` entries -- to recover the real base classes of
each class (single and multiple inheritance, with each base's byte offset
within the derived object). This is read-only enrichment: it does not create
or modify any IDA struct types, it only adds a comment at the vtable's
address, e.g.:

```
RTTI hierarchy: ClassName : BaseA (+0x8), BaseB (+0x18)   [multiple inheritance]
```

or for single inheritance:

```
RTTI hierarchy: ClassName : BaseClass
```

### Constructor-driven struct layout synthesis (opt-in review, not auto-applied)

Also independent of the two features above: for each class, the plugin
decompiles the same constructor candidate the singleton-chain scan already
settled on and walks its body for direct field-initialization writes on the
constructor's own `this` parameter -- either raw pointer-cast writes
(`*(_DWORD *)(a1 + 140) = 200;`) or writes Hex-Rays already rendered as
member accesses (`a1->field_8C = 200;`). From the recorded
`(offset, width, is_pointer)` triples it synthesizes a best-effort `struct`
type describing the class's memory layout: real fields named
`field_0x<offset>`, gaps between them filled with anonymous
`gap_0x<offset>` byte-array padding so the layout and total size stay
accurate even where the real field contents aren't known, and -- since the
class is already known to be polymorphic from the RTTI scan above -- an
explicit `vfptr` member at offset 0 when no write already covers it. If an
`operator new(SIZE)` call with a constant size can be found among the
constructor's direct callers, that size is used to add one final trailing
padding member so the struct's total size matches the real allocation.

If any two recorded field writes conflict (overlapping byte ranges with
different shapes), or a field's width isn't 1/2/4/8 bytes, the whole class
is dropped from struct synthesis for this class rather than guessing --
better no struct than a wrong one.

**This synthesized struct is registered into Local Types (as
`__sm_layout_<ClassName>`, with `::` replaced by `_`) and a comment is added
to the constructor pointing at it -- but it is never automatically applied to
any lvar, parameter, or global.** This is a deliberate safety choice: a
misclassified field width, an unrecognized write shape, or a genuinely
different memory layout in another constructor of the same class could
silently retype the object wrong everywhere it's used, corrupting
decompilation output well beyond the one function being analyzed. Reviewing
the generated type in Local Types and applying it by hand (right-click a
variable -> "Set lvar type") keeps a human in the loop for that judgment
call.

Both features run together in the same pass, and a scan log (written next to
the current database) records the resolved/unresolved outcome for each class
in both categories (including the struct layout synthesis).

## Requirements

- IDA Professional 9.2 or newer, Hex-Rays decompiler
- IDA SDK configured through the `IDASDK` environment variable (a checkout of
  [HexRaysSA/ida-sdk](https://github.com/HexRaysSA/ida-sdk) at a tag with
  CMake support)
- CMake 3.27+, Visual Studio with C++ build tools

## Build

```powershell
$env:IDASDK = "C:\path\to\ida-sdk"
cmake -S . -B build -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64
cmake --build build --config Release
```

See the top-level `IDA-Plugins/README.md` for the shared install script that
builds and deploys every plugin in this repo at once.

## Diagnostic log

Running the plugin writes `sm_global_finder_scan.log` next to the current
database, listing every scanned class's singleton-chain result, RTTI
hierarchy result, and struct-layout-synthesis result (resolved or, with a
reason, unresolved).

```
