`sm-global-finder/src/ctree_analysis.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "ctree_analysis.h"

#include <funcs.hpp>
#include <hexrays.hpp>

namespace
{

const cexpr_t *skip_casts(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  while ( e != nullptr && e->op == cot_cast )
    e = e->x;
  return e;
}

bool is_call_to(const cexpr_t *e, ea_t target_ea)
{
  e = skip_casts(e);
  if ( e == nullptr || e->op != cot_call )
    return false;
  const cexpr_t *callee = skip_casts(e->x);
  return callee != nullptr && callee->op == cot_obj && callee->obj_ea == target_ea;
}

// Finds the first "global = ctor_ea(...)" assignment in program order, either
// directly ("global = ctor(...)") or through one level of indirection
// ("local = ctor(...); ... ; global = local;" -- the common try/catch-wrapped
// or null-checked allocator shape).
struct find_global_store_visitor_t final : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  ea_t ctor_ea;
  ea_t found_global_ea = BADADDR;
  qvector<int> ctor_result_locals; // lvar indices holding ctor_ea's return value

  explicit find_global_store_visitor_t(ea_t ctor_ea_)
    : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST), ctor_ea(ctor_ea_) {}

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op != cot_asg )
      return 0;

    if ( is_call_to(e->y, ctor_ea) )
    {
      if ( e->x->op == cot_obj )
      {
        found_global_ea = e->x->obj_ea;
        return 1; // stop: first direct match in program order
      }
      if ( e->x->op == cot_var )
        ctor_result_locals.push_back(e->x->v.idx);
      return 0;
    }

    if ( e->x->op == cot_obj )
    {
      const cexpr_t *rhs = skip_casts(e->y);
      if ( rhs != nullptr && rhs->op == cot_var )
      {
        for ( int idx : ctor_result_locals )
        {
          if ( idx == rhs->v.idx )
          {
            found_global_ea = e->x->obj_ea;
            return 1; // stop: first indirect match in program order
          }
        }
      }
    }

    return 0;
  }
};

// Finds the first "global = this;" self-registration assignment in program
// order, inside the constructor's own body (this_lvar_idx is the ctor's
// "this" argument's index into cfunc->lvars). Mirrors the shape/style of
// find_global_store_visitor_t above, but looks at a var (the ctor's own
// argument), never a call result.
struct find_self_registered_global_visitor_t final : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  int this_lvar_idx;
  ea_t found_global_ea = BADADDR;

  explicit find_self_registered_global_visitor_t(int this_lvar_idx_)
    : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST), this_lvar_idx(this_lvar_idx_) {}

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op != cot_asg || e->x->op != cot_obj )
      return 0;

    const cexpr_t *rhs = skip_casts(e->y);
    if ( rhs != nullptr && rhs->op == cot_var && rhs->v.idx == this_lvar_idx )
    {
      found_global_ea = e->x->obj_ea;
      return 1; // stop: first match in program order
    }

    return 0;
  }
};

} // namespace

ea_t find_self_registered_global(ea_t ctor_ea, qstring *reason)
{
  func_t *pfn = get_func(ctor_ea);
  if ( pfn == nullptr )
  {
    if ( reason != nullptr )
      *reason = "constructor candidate has no containing function";
    return BADADDR;
  }

  hexrays_failure_t hf;
  cfuncptr_t cf = decompile_func(pfn, &hf);
  if ( cf == nullptr )
  {
    if ( reason != nullptr )
      *reason = "decompilation of constructor candidate failed";
    return BADADDR;
  }

  // Locate the ctor's "this" argument: by MSVC __fastcall/__thiscall
  // convention, this is the first (lowest-index) argument lvar.
  lvars_t *lvars = cf->get_lvars();
  int this_lvar_idx = -1;
  if ( lvars != nullptr )
  {
    for ( size_t i = 0; i < lvars->size(); ++i )
    {
      if ( (*lvars)[i].is_arg_var() )
      {
        this_lvar_idx = int(i);
        break;
      }
    }
  }

  if ( this_lvar_idx < 0 )
  {
    if ( reason != nullptr )
      *reason = "constructor has no argument lvars (no 'this' parameter found)";
    return BADADDR;
  }

  find_self_registered_global_visitor_t visitor(this_lvar_idx);
  visitor.apply_to(&cf->body, nullptr);

  if ( visitor.found_global_ea == BADADDR && reason != nullptr )
    *reason = "constructor's own body has no self-registration store of its 'this' argument to a global";

  return visitor.found_global_ea;
}

ea_t find_global_store(ea_t allocator_ea, ea_t ctor_ea, qstring *reason)
{
  func_t *pfn = get_func(allocator_ea);
  if ( pfn == nullptr )
  {
    if ( reason != nullptr )
      *reason = "allocator candidate has no containing function";
    return BADADDR;
  }

  hexrays_failure_t hf;
  cfuncptr_t cf = decompile_func(pfn, &hf);
  if ( cf == nullptr )
  {
    if ( reason != nullptr )
      *reason = "decompilation of allocator candidate failed";
    return BADADDR;
  }

  find_global_store_visitor_t visitor(ctor_ea);
  visitor.apply_to(&cf->body, nullptr);

  if ( visitor.found_global_ea == BADADDR && reason != nullptr )
    *reason = "no direct or 1-level-indirect global store of ctor's return value found";

  return visitor.found_global_ea;
}

```

`sm-global-finder/src/ctree_analysis.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>

// Decompile allocator_ea and look for the first assignment, occurring after the
// call to ctor_ea, of ctor_ea's return value into a fixed-address global
// (cot_obj lvalue) -- never a stack local, struct field, or array index.
//
// Returns the global's ea on success, or BADADDR with *reason filled on failure.
ea_t find_global_store(ea_t allocator_ea, ea_t ctor_ea, qstring *reason);

// Decompile ctor_ea itself and look for the first assignment, in program
// order, of ctor_ea's own "this" argument (its first argument lvar, per
// MSVC __fastcall/__thiscall convention) into a fixed-address global
// (cot_obj lvalue) -- i.e. "g_Foo = this;" written directly inside the
// constructor's own body, after stripping casts on the RHS.
//
// This covers the "constructor self-registration" idiom, which in practice
// is the dominant singleton shape in this binary: the ctor itself stashes
// `this` into a global, rather than some caller storing the ctor's return
// value (that shape is what find_global_store() above looks for instead).
//
// Returns the global's ea on success, or BADADDR with *reason filled on failure
// (no containing function, decompilation failure, no argument lvars found, or
// no matching self-store located anywhere in the ctor's body).
ea_t find_self_registered_global(ea_t ctor_ea, qstring *reason);

```

`sm-global-finder/src/plugin_main.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "rtti_scan.h"
#include "rtti_hierarchy.h"
#include "ctree_analysis.h"
#include "struct_synth.h"

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>
#include <idp.hpp>
#include <loader.hpp>
#include <kernwin.hpp>
#include <name.hpp>
#include <funcs.hpp>
#include <hexrays.hpp>
#include <fpro.h>
#include <bytes.hpp>

namespace
{

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Results chooser
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct scan_results_popup_t final : chooser_t
{
  static const int widths[];
  static const char *const headers[];

  const qvector<scan_result_t> &results;

  explicit scan_results_popup_t(const qvector<scan_result_t> &results_)
    : chooser_t(
        CH_MODAL | CH_KEEP,
        6,
        widths,
        headers,
        "SM Global Finder Results"),
      results(results_)
  {
  }

  size_t idaapi get_count() const override
  {
    return results.size();
  }

  ea_t idaapi get_ea(size_t n) const override
  {
    const scan_result_t &r = results[n];
    return r.status == chain_status_t::resolved ? r.allocator_ea : r.vft_ea;
  }

  void idaapi get_row(
        qstrvec_t *cols_,
        int *,
        chooser_item_attrs_t *,
        size_t n) const override
  {
    const scan_result_t &r = results[n];
    qstrvec_t &cols = *cols_;
    cols[0] = r.class_name;
    cols[1].sprnt("%a", r.vft_ea);
    if ( r.status == chain_status_t::resolved )
    {
      cols[2].sprnt("%a", r.ctor_ea);
      cols[3].sprnt("%a", r.allocator_ea);
      cols[4].sprnt("%a", r.global_ea);
      cols[5] = "resolved";
    }
    else
    {
      if ( r.ctor_ea == BADADDR )
        cols[2] = "-";
      else
        cols[2].sprnt("%a", r.ctor_ea);
      cols[3] = "-";
      cols[4] = "-";
      cols[5].sprnt("unresolved: %s", r.reason.c_str());
    }
  }
};

const int scan_results_popup_t::widths[] =
{
  24,
  CHCOL_HEX | 16,
  CHCOL_HEX | 16,
  CHCOL_HEX | 16,
  CHCOL_HEX | 16,
  50,
};

const char *const scan_results_popup_t::headers[] =
{
  "Class",
  "VTable",
  "Constructor",
  "Allocator",
  "Global",
  "Status",
};

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Scan pipeline: vft -> ctor -> allocator -> global, with bounded hop search.
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

constexpr int MAX_ALLOCATOR_HOPS = 2;

// Tries the "constructor self-registration" shape first: the ctor itself
// stores its own "this" argument into a global (verified against the real
// binary to be the dominant singleton idiom here -- see ctree_analysis.h).
// If the ctor's body has such a store, we still need something to rename
// as "ClassName::create"; the most useful candidate is a real caller
// of the ctor (the allocator wrapper that does `operator new` + call ctor,
// unconditionally, with no check of whether the global is already set --
// so it is a plain factory, not a lazy singleton accessor).
// If the ctor has no callers at all, there is nothing sane to rename as the
// allocator distinct from the ctor itself, so this path reports failure and
// lets the caller fall back to resolve_allocator_chain() instead of aliasing
// create onto the same address as the ctor (which would just clobber
// the ctor's own rename).
bool resolve_self_registration(ea_t ctor_ea, scan_result_t *result, qstring *out_reason)
{
  qstring why;
  ea_t global_ea = find_self_registered_global(ctor_ea, &why);
  if ( global_ea == BADADDR )
  {
    *out_reason = why;
    return false;
  }

  qvector<ea_t> callers = find_callers_of(ctor_ea);
  if ( callers.empty() )
  {
    *out_reason = "self-registration found a global store but constructor has no call xrefs to name as an allocator";
    return false;
  }

  result->ctor_ea = ctor_ea;
  result->allocator_ea = callers[0];
  result->global_ea = global_ea;
  result->status = chain_status_t::resolved;
  return true;
}

// Tries to resolve the allocator+global for one specific ctor candidate,
// searching up to MAX_ALLOCATOR_HOPS levels of callers.
bool resolve_allocator_chain(ea_t ctor_ea, scan_result_t *result, qstring *out_reason)
{
  qvector<ea_t> frontier = find_callers_of(ctor_ea);
  if ( frontier.empty() )
  {
    *out_reason = "constructor has no call xrefs";
    return false;
  }

  qstring last_reason = "no global store found within max call hops";
  for ( int depth = 0; depth < MAX_ALLOCATOR_HOPS && !frontier.empty(); ++depth )
  {
    qvector<ea_t> next_frontier;
    for ( ea_t candidate : frontier )
    {
      qstring why;
      ea_t global_ea = find_global_store(candidate, ctor_ea, &why);
      if ( global_ea != BADADDR )
      {
        result->ctor_ea = ctor_ea;
        result->allocator_ea = candidate;
        result->global_ea = global_ea;
        result->status = chain_status_t::resolved;
        return true;
      }
      last_reason = why;

      for ( ea_t caller : find_callers_of(candidate) )
        next_frontier.push_back(caller);
    }
    frontier.swap(next_frontier);
  }

  *out_reason = last_reason;
  return false;
}

scan_result_t analyze_class(const rtti_vtable_t &vt)
{
  scan_result_t result;
  result.class_name = vt.class_name;
  result.vft_ea = vt.vft_ea;

  qstring reason;
  qvector<ea_t> ctor_candidates = find_ctor_candidates_for_vtable(vt.vft_ea, &reason);
  if ( ctor_candidates.empty() )
  {
    result.reason = reason;
    return result;
  }

  // A class commonly has several constructors (default/copy/move/...) that
  // all stamp the same vtable pointer -- try each until one leads to a
  // resolvable allocator+global chain.
  qstring last_reason;
  for ( ea_t ctor_ea : ctor_candidates )
  {
    // Self-registration (ctor stores its own "this" into a global) is the
    // dominant real-world shape in this binary -- try it before the
    // caller-search chain below.
    qstring self_why;
    if ( resolve_self_registration(ctor_ea, &result, &self_why) )
      return result;

    qstring chain_why;
    if ( resolve_allocator_chain(ctor_ea, &result, &chain_why) )
      return result;

    last_reason.sprnt(
        "self-registration: %s; caller-search: %s",
        self_why.c_str(),
        chain_why.c_str());
  }

  result.ctor_ea = ctor_candidates[0];
  result.reason.sprnt(
      "%s (tried %d ctor candidate%s)",
      last_reason.c_str(),
      int(ctor_candidates.size()),
      ctor_candidates.size() == 1 ? "" : "s");
  return result;
}

void apply_result(const scan_result_t &r)
{
  if ( r.status != chain_status_t::resolved )
    return;

  qstring ctor_name;
  ctor_name.sprnt("%s::ctor", r.class_name.c_str());
  set_name(r.ctor_ea, ctor_name.c_str(), SN_FORCE | SN_NOWARN);

  qstring alloc_name;
  alloc_name.sprnt("%s::create", r.class_name.c_str());
  set_name(r.allocator_ea, alloc_name.c_str(), SN_FORCE | SN_NOWARN);

  qstring global_name;
  global_name.sprnt("g_%s", r.class_name.c_str());
  set_name(r.global_ea, global_name.c_str(), SN_FORCE | SN_NOWARN);

  qstring cmt;
  cmt.sprnt(
      "Singleton pattern: constructs %s, global at %s (%a)",
      r.class_name.c_str(),
      global_name.c_str(),
      r.global_ea);

  func_t *ctor_fn = get_func(r.ctor_ea);
  if ( ctor_fn != nullptr )
    set_func_cmt(ctor_fn, cmt.c_str(), true);

  func_t *alloc_fn = get_func(r.allocator_ea);
  if ( alloc_fn != nullptr )
    set_func_cmt(alloc_fn, cmt.c_str(), true);
}

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// RTTI class hierarchy enrichment: independent of the singleton chain scan
// above -- walks the real MSVC RTTI structures (Complete Object Locator ->
// Class Hierarchy Descriptor -> base-class array) for each vtable already
// found by find_rtti_vtables() and leaves a descriptive comment at the
// vtable's address.
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

qstring build_hierarchy_comment(const hierarchy_info_t &h)
{
  qstring cmt;
  cmt.sprnt("RTTI hierarchy: %s :", h.class_name.c_str());

  bool show_offsets = h.bases.size() > 1 || h.is_multiple_inheritance;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < h.bases.size(); ++i )
  {
    const base_class_info_t &b = h.bases[i];
    if ( i > 0 )
      cmt += ",";
    cmt += " ";
    cmt += b.class_name;
    if ( show_offsets )
    {
      qstring off;
      off.sprnt(" (+0x%x)", b.mdisp);
      cmt += off;
    }
    if ( b.is_virtual )
      cmt += " [virtual]";
  }

  if ( h.is_multiple_inheritance )
    cmt += "   [multiple inheritance]";

  return cmt;
}

void apply_hierarchy_comment(const hierarchy_info_t &h)
{
  qstring cmt = build_hierarchy_comment(h);
  set_cmt(h.vft_ea, cmt.c_str(), false);
}

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Constructor-driven struct layout synthesis: also independent of the
// singleton chain scan above -- decompiles each class's ctor candidate
// (whichever one the singleton-chain scan above already settled on, so we
// don't re-derive/re-guess which candidate to use) and, on success, leaves
// a comment on the constructor pointing at the synthesized Local Type. See
// struct_synth.h/.cpp for why this never auto-applies the type anywhere.
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

void apply_layout_result(const layout_synth_result_t &l)
{
  qstring cmt;
  cmt.sprnt(
      "Struct layout guess synthesized from this constructor's field writes -- "
      "see Local Type '%s' (not auto-applied; review and apply manually).",
      l.type_name.c_str());

  func_t *ctor_fn = get_func(l.ctor_ea);
  if ( ctor_fn != nullptr )
    set_func_cmt(ctor_fn, cmt.c_str(), true);
}

// Writes one line per scanned class to a log file next to the current
// database, so results survive after the modal chooser is closed.
qstring write_scan_log(
    const qvector<scan_result_t> &results,
    const qvector<hierarchy_info_t> &hierarchies,
    const qvector<rtti_vtable_t> &unresolved_hierarchy_vtables,
    const qvector<layout_synth_result_t> &layouts)
{
  char dir[QMAXPATH];
  qdirname(dir, sizeof(dir), get_path(PATH_TYPE_IDB));

  qstring log_path;
  log_path.sprnt("%s/sm_global_finder_scan.log", dir);

  FILE *fp = qfopen(log_path.c_str(), "w");
  if ( fp == nullptr )
    return qstring();

  for ( const scan_result_t &r : results )
  {
    if ( r.status == chain_status_t::resolved )
    {
      qfprintf(
          fp,
          "RESOLVED   %-40s vft=%a ctor=%a allocator=%a global=%a\n",
          r.class_name.c_str(),
          r.vft_ea,
          r.ctor_ea,
          r.allocator_ea,
          r.global_ea);
    }
    else
    {
      qfprintf(
          fp,
          "UNRESOLVED %-40s vft=%a reason=%s\n",
          r.class_name.c_str(),
          r.vft_ea,
          r.reason.c_str());
    }
  }

  qfprintf(fp, "\n--- RTTI class hierarchy resolution ---\n");
  for ( const hierarchy_info_t &h : hierarchies )
  {
    qfprintf(
        fp,
        "HIERARCHY  %-40s vft=%a %s\n",
        h.class_name.c_str(),
        h.vft_ea,
        build_hierarchy_comment(h).c_str());
  }
  for ( const rtti_vtable_t &vt : unresolved_hierarchy_vtables )
  {
    qfprintf(
        fp,
        "HIERARCHY  %-40s vft=%a unresolved\n",
        vt.class_name.c_str(),
        vt.vft_ea);
  }

  qfprintf(fp, "\n--- Struct layout synthesis ---\n");
  for ( const layout_synth_result_t &l : layouts )
  {
    if ( l.resolved )
    {
      qfprintf(
          fp,
          "LAYOUT     %-40s ctor=%a type=%s\n",
          l.class_name.c_str(),
          l.ctor_ea,
          l.type_name.c_str());
    }
    else
    {
      qfprintf(
          fp,
          "LAYOUT     %-40s ctor=%a reason=%s\n",
          l.class_name.c_str(),
          l.ctor_ea,
          l.reason.c_str());
    }
  }

  qfclose(fp);
  return log_path;
}

bool idaapi run(size_t)
{
  qvector<rtti_vtable_t> vtables = find_rtti_vtables();
  if ( vtables.empty() )
  {
    warning("SM Global Finder: no MSVC RTTI vtables (??_7...@@6B@) found in this database.");
    return true;
  }

  qvector<scan_result_t> results;
  results.reserve(vtables.size());
  size_t resolved_count = 0;

  qvector<hierarchy_info_t> hierarchies;
  qvector<rtti_vtable_t> unresolved_hierarchy_vtables;
  size_t hierarchy_resolved_count = 0;

  qvector<layout_synth_result_t> layouts;
  size_t layout_resolved_count = 0;

  for ( const rtti_vtable_t &vt : vtables )
  {
    scan_result_t r = analyze_class(vt);
    if ( r.status == chain_status_t::resolved )
    {
      apply_result(r);
      ++resolved_count;
    }
    results.push_back(r);

    // Independent RTTI hierarchy enrichment -- runs for every discovered
    // vtable regardless of whether the singleton chain above resolved.
    hierarchy_info_t hinfo;
    qstring hreason;
    if ( resolve_class_hierarchy(vt.vft_ea, &hinfo, &hreason) )
    {
      apply_hierarchy_comment(hinfo);
      hierarchies.push_back(hinfo);
      ++hierarchy_resolved_count;
    }
    else
    {
      unresolved_hierarchy_vtables.push_back(vt);
    }

    // Independent struct layout synthesis -- reuses whichever ctor
    // candidate the singleton-chain scan above already settled on (r.ctor_ea
    // is only BADADDR when find_ctor_candidates_for_vtable() found nothing
    // at all, in which case there's no ctor to decompile here either).
    if ( r.ctor_ea != BADADDR )
    {
      layout_synth_result_t lresult = synthesize_struct_layout(
          r.ctor_ea,
          vt.class_name,
          vt.vft_ea,
          true /* is_polymorphic: vt came from find_rtti_vtables() */);
      if ( lresult.resolved )
      {
        apply_layout_result(lresult);
        ++layout_resolved_count;
      }
      layouts.push_back(lresult);
    }
  }

  qstring log_path = write_scan_log(results, hierarchies, unresolved_hierarchy_vtables, layouts);

  msg(
      "SM Global Finder: scanned %d classes, resolved %d, unresolved %d. "
      "RTTI hierarchies resolved %d/%d. Struct layouts synthesized %d/%d. Log: %s\n",
      int(results.size()),
      int(resolved_count),
      int(results.size() - resolved_count),
      int(hierarchy_resolved_count),
      int(vtables.size()),
      int(layout_resolved_count),
      int(vtables.size()),
      log_path.empty() ? "<failed to write>" : log_path.c_str());

  scan_results_popup_t popup(results);
  popup.choose();
  return true;
}

plugmod_t *idaapi init()
{
  if ( !init_hexrays_plugin() )
    return nullptr;
  return PLUGIN_OK;
}

} // namespace

plugin_t PLUGIN =
{
  IDP_INTERFACE_VERSION,
  0,
  init,
  nullptr,
  run,
  "Scans MSVC RTTI vtables for the vft->ctor->allocator->global singleton pattern and renames the discovered chain.",
  "",
  "SM Global Finder",
  ""
};

```

`sm-global-finder/src/rtti_hierarchy.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "rtti_hierarchy.h"

#include <bytes.hpp>

namespace
{

constexpr uint32 MAX_BASE_CLASSES = 64;
constexpr size_t MAX_TYPE_NAME_LEN = 512;

// x64 MSVC RTTI layout (all image-relative offsets are 32-bit DWORDs stored
// relative to the module's image base -- see rtti_hierarchy.h / the task
// spec this mirrors).
struct col_raw_t
{
  uint32 signature;
  uint32 offset;
  uint32 cdOffset;
  uint32 pTypeDescriptorOffset;
  uint32 pClassDescriptorOffset;
  uint32 pSelfOffset;
};

struct chd_raw_t
{
  uint32 signature;
  uint32 attributes;
  uint32 numBaseClasses;
  uint32 pBaseClassArrayOffset;
};

struct bcd_raw_t
{
  uint32 pTypeDescriptorOffset;
  uint32 numContainedBases;
  uint32 mdisp;
  uint32 pdisp;
  uint32 vbptrOffset;
  uint32 attributes;
};

bool read_dword_checked(ea_t ea, uint32 *out)
{
  if ( !is_mapped(ea) )
    return false;
  *out = get_dword(ea);
  return true;
}

bool read_qword_checked(ea_t ea, uint64 *out)
{
  if ( !is_mapped(ea) )
    return false;
  *out = get_qword(ea);
  return true;
}

bool read_col(ea_t ea, col_raw_t *out)
{
  return read_dword_checked(ea + 0, &out->signature)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 4, &out->offset)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 8, &out->cdOffset)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 12, &out->pTypeDescriptorOffset)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 16, &out->pClassDescriptorOffset)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 20, &out->pSelfOffset);
}

bool read_chd(ea_t ea, chd_raw_t *out)
{
  return read_dword_checked(ea + 0, &out->signature)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 4, &out->attributes)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 8, &out->numBaseClasses)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 12, &out->pBaseClassArrayOffset);
}

bool read_bcd(ea_t ea, bcd_raw_t *out)
{
  return read_dword_checked(ea + 0, &out->pTypeDescriptorOffset)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 4, &out->numContainedBases)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 8, &out->mdisp)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 12, &out->pdisp)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 16, &out->vbptrOffset)
      && read_dword_checked(ea + 20, &out->attributes);
}

// Reads a NUL-terminated byte string starting at ea, bailing defensively if
// any byte is unmapped or the string runs past a sane cap.
bool read_cstr_at(ea_t ea, qstring *out, size_t max_len = MAX_TYPE_NAME_LEN)
{
  out->qclear();
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < max_len; ++i )
  {
    ea_t cur = ea + i;
    if ( !is_mapped(cur) )
      return false;
    uchar b = get_byte(cur);
    if ( b == 0 )
      return true;
    out->append(char(b));
  }
  return true; // hit the cap without a NUL -- still usable, just truncated.
}

// Extracts the real class name out of a _TypeDescriptor's embedded mangled
// name, e.g. ".?AVClassName@@" (class) or ".?AUClassName@@" (struct).
// Namespace-qualified names are stored most-nested-first and '@'-separated,
// e.g. ".?AVClassName@Outer@Inner@@" means "Inner::Outer::ClassName" --
// mirrors extract_class_name()'s approach in rtti_scan.cpp, adapted for this
// different mangled-name shape.
//
// NOTE: qstring::substr(pos, end) takes an *ending index*, not a length
// (unlike std::string::substr) -- every call below passes an end index.
qstring extract_class_name_from_type_descriptor(const qstring &raw_name)
{
  bool is_class_or_struct =
      raw_name.length() > 4
      && raw_name[0] == '.'
      && raw_name[1] == '?'
      && raw_name[2] == 'A'
      && (raw_name[3] == 'V' || raw_name[3] == 'U');
  if ( !is_class_or_struct )
    return raw_name; // unexpected shape -- return as-is so it's still visible

  qstring body = raw_name.substr(4, raw_name.length());
  if ( body.length() >= 2
    && body[body.length() - 2] == '@'
    && body[body.length() - 1] == '@' )
  {
    body = body.substr(0, body.length() - 2);
  }
  if ( body.empty() )
    return body;

  qvector<qstring> parts;
  size_t start = 0;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i <= body.length(); ++i )
  {
    if ( i == body.length() || body[i] == '@' )
    {
      parts.push_back(body.substr(start, i));
      start = i + 1;
    }
  }

  qstring result;
  for ( size_t i = parts.size(); i-- > 0; )
  {
    if ( !result.empty() )
      result += "::";
    result += parts[i];
  }
  return result;
}

bool resolve_type_descriptor_name(ea_t image_base, uint32 offset, qstring *out_name, qstring *reason)
{
  if ( offset == 0 )
  {
    *reason = "type descriptor offset is 0";
    return false;
  }
  ea_t td_ea = image_base + offset;
  if ( !is_mapped(td_ea) )
  {
    *reason = "type descriptor address is unmapped";
    return false;
  }

  // _TypeDescriptor layout: QWORD pVFTable, QWORD spare, char name[].
  ea_t name_ea = td_ea + 16;
  qstring raw_name;
  if ( !read_cstr_at(name_ea, &raw_name) || raw_name.empty() )
  {
    *reason = "failed to read type descriptor name string";
    return false;
  }

  *out_name = extract_class_name_from_type_descriptor(raw_name);
  if ( out_name->empty() )
  {
    *reason = "extracted class name was empty";
    return false;
  }
  return true;
}

} // namespace

bool resolve_class_hierarchy(ea_t vft_ea, hierarchy_info_t *out, qstring *reason)
{
  if ( vft_ea == BADADDR )
  {
    *reason = "invalid vtable address";
    return false;
  }

  // The RTTI Complete Object Locator pointer sits at vft_ea - 8, immediately
  // before the vtable's first virtual-function pointer.
  ea_t col_ptr_ea = vft_ea - 8;
  uint64 col_ea_raw = 0;
  if ( !read_qword_checked(col_ptr_ea, &col_ea_raw) || col_ea_raw == 0 )
  {
    *reason = "could not read _RTTICompleteObjectLocator pointer at vft-8";
    return false;
  }
  ea_t col_ea = ea_t(col_ea_raw);
  if ( !is_mapped(col_ea) )
  {
    *reason = "_RTTICompleteObjectLocator address is unmapped";
    return false;
  }

  col_raw_t col;
  if ( !read_col(col_ea, &col) )
  {
    *reason = "failed to read _RTTICompleteObjectLocator fields";
    return false;
  }

  ea_t image_base = get_imagebase();

  qstring class_name;
  qstring name_reason;
  if ( !resolve_type_descriptor_name(image_base, col.pTypeDescriptorOffset, &class_name, &name_reason) )
  {
    *reason = name_reason;
    return false;
  }

  if ( col.pClassDescriptorOffset == 0 )
  {
    *reason = "class hierarchy descriptor offset is 0";
    return false;
  }
  ea_t chd_ea = image_base + col.pClassDescriptorOffset;
  if ( !is_mapped(chd_ea) )
  {
    *reason = "_RTTIClassHierarchyDescriptor address is unmapped";
    return false;
  }

  chd_raw_t chd;
  if ( !read_chd(chd_ea, &chd) )
  {
    *reason = "failed to read _RTTIClassHierarchyDescriptor fields";
    return false;
  }

  if ( chd.numBaseClasses == 0 || chd.numBaseClasses > MAX_BASE_CLASSES )
  {
    *reason = "numBaseClasses is 0 or unreasonably large";
    return false;
  }

  if ( chd.pBaseClassArrayOffset == 0 )
  {
    *reason = "base class array offset is 0";
    return false;
  }
  ea_t base_array_ea = image_base + chd.pBaseClassArrayOffset;

  qvector<base_class_info_t> bases;
  for ( uint32 i = 0; i < chd.numBaseClasses; ++i )
  {
    ea_t slot_ea = base_array_ea + i * 4;
    uint32 desc_offset = 0;
    if ( !read_dword_checked(slot_ea, &desc_offset) || desc_offset == 0 )
    {
      *reason = "failed to read base-class array entry";
      return false;
    }

    ea_t bcd_ea = image_base + desc_offset;
    if ( !is_mapped(bcd_ea) )
    {
      *reason = "_RTTIBaseClassDescriptor address is unmapped";
      return false;
    }

    bcd_raw_t bcd;
    if ( !read_bcd(bcd_ea, &bcd) )
    {
      *reason = "failed to read _RTTIBaseClassDescriptor fields";
      return false;
    }

    // Index 0 is always the class itself -- already captured via the COL's
    // own type descriptor above, so skip it here.
    if ( i == 0 )
      continue;

    qstring base_name;
    qstring base_name_reason;
    if ( !resolve_type_descriptor_name(image_base, bcd.pTypeDescriptorOffset, &base_name, &base_name_reason) )
    {
      *reason = base_name_reason;
      return false;
    }

    base_class_info_t base;
    base.class_name = base_name;
    base.mdisp = int32(bcd.mdisp);
    // Per the standard MSVC ABI convention: pdisp == -1 means this base is
    // reached by a plain member displacement (mdisp); any other value means
    // it's reached through a vbtable, i.e. virtual inheritance.
    base.is_virtual = int32(bcd.pdisp) != -1;
    bases.push_back(base);
  }

  out->class_name = class_name;
  out->vft_ea = vft_ea;
  out->bases = bases;
  out->is_multiple_inheritance = (chd.attributes & 1) != 0;
  return true;
}

```

`sm-global-finder/src/rtti_hierarchy.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>
#include <idp.hpp>

// One base class contribution to a derived class's hierarchy, resolved from
// the base-class array of a _RTTIClassHierarchyDescriptor (index 1..N-1;
// index 0, the class itself, is excluded before this struct is populated).
struct base_class_info_t
{
  qstring class_name;
  int32 mdisp = 0;      // byte displacement of this base within the derived object
  bool is_virtual = false;
};

// Result of walking the MSVC RTTI Complete Object Locator chain for one
// vtable: COL -> _RTTIClassHierarchyDescriptor -> base-class array ->
// _RTTIBaseClassDescriptor[] -> _TypeDescriptor names.
struct hierarchy_info_t
{
  qstring class_name;
  ea_t vft_ea = BADADDR;
  qvector<base_class_info_t> bases; // direct + indirect bases, self excluded
  bool is_multiple_inheritance = false;
};

// Walks the MSVC x64 RTTI structures rooted at the _RTTICompleteObjectLocator
// pointer stored at vft_ea - 8 (see rtti_hierarchy.cpp for the full layout
// this mirrors) and fills *out with the resolved class hierarchy.
//
// Returns false (and fills *reason) if the COL pointer, class hierarchy
// descriptor, base-class array, or any base-class descriptor cannot be read
// or looks unreasonable (e.g. an out-of-range base-class count).
bool resolve_class_hierarchy(ea_t vft_ea, hierarchy_info_t *out, qstring *reason);

```

`sm-global-finder/src/rtti_scan.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "rtti_scan.h"

#include <name.hpp>
#include <demangle.hpp>
#include <xref.hpp>
#include <funcs.hpp>

#include <qset.hpp>

#include <cstring>

namespace
{

bool has_rtti_vtable_shape(const char *name)
{
  // Matches "??_7<ClassName>@@6B@"
  if ( qstrncmp(name, "??_7", 4) != 0 )
    return false;
  size_t len = qstrlen(name);
  static const char suffix[] = "@@6B@";
  size_t suffix_len = sizeof(suffix) - 1;
  if ( len <= 4 + suffix_len )
    return false;
  return qstrcmp(name + len - suffix_len, suffix) == 0;
}

// std:: (and other STL/CRT) types and class templates are never the kind of
// hand-written singleton this plugin looks for -- skip them so the scan
// doesn't waste time decompiling them and doesn't produce garbage class
// names out of deeply nested template argument lists.
bool is_noise_vtable(const char *mangled_name)
{
  return strstr(mangled_name, "?$") != nullptr    // class template instantiation
      || strstr(mangled_name, "@std@@") != nullptr; // std:: namespace
}

// Extract "<ClassName>" out of a demangled "const ClassName::`vftable'" string,
// falling back to a manual strip of the mangled name if demangling fails.
qstring extract_class_name(const char *mangled)
{
  qstring demangled;
  int32 code = demangle_name(&demangled, mangled, MNG_SHORT_FORM);
  if ( code >= 0 )
  {
    // Typical shapes: "ClassName::`vftable'" or "const ClassName::`vftable'"
    size_t pos = demangled.find("::`vftable'");
    if ( pos != qstring::npos )
    {
      qstring cls = demangled.substr(0, pos);
      size_t space = cls.rfind(' ');
      if ( space != qstring::npos )
        // NOTE: qstring::substr(pos, n) takes an *ending index*, not a
        // length (unlike std::string::substr) -- so the end argument here
        // must be cls.length(), not a remaining-character count.
        cls = cls.substr(space + 1, cls.length());
      if ( !cls.empty() )
        return cls;
    }
  }

  // Fallback: strip "??_7" prefix and "@@6B@" suffix directly from the mangled name.
  qstring raw = mangled;
  // See note above: qstring::substr's 2nd arg is an ending index, so pass
  // raw.length() (the full end), not raw.length()-4.
  raw = raw.substr(4, raw.length());
  size_t suffix_pos = raw.find("@@6B@");
  if ( suffix_pos != qstring::npos )
    raw = raw.substr(0, suffix_pos);
  return raw;
}

} // namespace

qvector<rtti_vtable_t> find_rtti_vtables()
{
  qvector<rtti_vtable_t> out;
  size_t cnt = get_nlist_size();
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < cnt; ++i )
  {
    const char *name = get_nlist_name(i);
    if ( name == nullptr || !has_rtti_vtable_shape(name) || is_noise_vtable(name) )
      continue;

    rtti_vtable_t entry;
    entry.vft_ea = get_nlist_ea(i);
    entry.class_name = extract_class_name(name);
    if ( entry.vft_ea != BADADDR && !entry.class_name.empty() )
      out.push_back(entry);
  }
  return out;
}

qvector<ea_t> find_ctor_candidates_for_vtable(ea_t vft_ea, qstring *reason)
{
  qvector<ea_t> candidates;
  qset<ea_t> seen;
  xrefblk_t xb;
  for ( bool ok = xb.first_to(vft_ea, XREF_DATA); ok; ok = xb.next_to() )
  {
    func_t *pfn = get_func(xb.from);
    if ( pfn == nullptr )
      continue;
    ea_t start = pfn->start_ea;
    if ( seen.find(start) == seen.end() )
    {
      seen.insert(start);
      candidates.push_back(start);
    }
  }

  if ( candidates.empty() && reason != nullptr )
    *reason = "no data xrefs to vtable resolve to a containing function";

  return candidates;
}

qvector<ea_t> find_callers_of(ea_t target_ea)
{
  qvector<ea_t> out;
  qset<ea_t> seen;
  xrefblk_t xb;
  for ( bool ok = xb.first_to(target_ea, XREF_ALL); ok; ok = xb.next_to() )
  {
    if ( xb.type != fl_CN && xb.type != fl_CF )
      continue;
    func_t *pfn = get_func(xb.from);
    if ( pfn == nullptr )
      continue;
    ea_t start = pfn->start_ea;
    if ( seen.find(start) == seen.end() )
    {
      seen.insert(start);
      out.push_back(start);
    }
  }
  return out;
}

```

`sm-global-finder/src/rtti_scan.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>
#include <idp.hpp>

// One MSVC RTTI vtable ("??_7<ClassName>@@6B@") found in the database.
struct rtti_vtable_t
{
  qstring class_name;
  ea_t vft_ea = BADADDR;
};

enum class chain_status_t
{
  resolved,
  unresolved,
};

// Result of walking vft -> ctor -> allocator -> global for one class.
struct scan_result_t
{
  qstring class_name;
  ea_t vft_ea = BADADDR;
  ea_t ctor_ea = BADADDR;
  ea_t allocator_ea = BADADDR;
  ea_t global_ea = BADADDR;
  chain_status_t status = chain_status_t::unresolved;
  qstring reason; // populated when status == unresolved
};

// Enumerate every public name matching "??_7<ClassName>@@6B@".
qvector<rtti_vtable_t> find_rtti_vtables();

// Find every distinct function that writes vft_ea into an object (candidate
// constructors -- a class commonly has several: default/copy/move/etc, all
// stamping the same vtable pointer). Empty on failure, with *reason filled.
qvector<ea_t> find_ctor_candidates_for_vtable(ea_t vft_ea, qstring *reason);

// Find the candidate caller functions of ctor_ea (functions containing a call to it).
qvector<ea_t> find_callers_of(ea_t target_ea);

```

`sm-global-finder/src/struct_synth.cpp`:

```cpp
// Constructor-driven struct layout synthesis (conservative,
// read-only-application v1).
//
// For each class this plugin already resolved a ctor candidate for, we
// decompile that ctor and walk its body for direct field-initialization
// writes on the ctor's own "this" parameter -- either the raw-pointer-cast
// shape Hex-Rays sometimes leaves un-simplified ("*(_DWORD *)(a1 + 140) =
// 200;") or the already-member-accessed shape it produces once it infers a
// partial struct ("a1->field_8C = 200;"). From those writes we synthesize a
// best-effort `struct` type and register it into Local Types -- but we
// deliberately never retype any lvar/parameter/global with it. A wrong
// guess (a misclassified field width, or two constructors of the same class
// writing genuinely different shapes) would silently corrupt decompilation
// everywhere that type got applied; leaving it as a reviewable Local Type
// the user can inspect and apply by hand (right-click "Set lvar type") is
// the safe default for a v1 heuristic like this.

#include "struct_synth.h"
#include "rtti_scan.h" // find_callers_of()

#include <funcs.hpp>
#include <hexrays.hpp>
#include <typeinf.hpp>
#include <name.hpp>

#include <algorithm>

namespace
{

constexpr const char *SYNTHETIC_PREFIX = "__sm_layout_";

const cexpr_t *skip_casts(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  while ( e != nullptr && e->op == cot_cast )
    e = e->x;
  return e;
}

// One recorded direct field write on the ctor's "this" parameter.
struct field_write_t
{
  uint64 offset = 0;
  int width = 0;
  bool is_pointer = false;
};

// Finds every "*(TYPE*)(this + N) = expr;" / "this->field = expr;" style
// write in ctor_ea's body, recording (offset, width, is_pointer) for each.
// Anything not cleanly shaped as "this + literal" (another variable, a
// multiply/array index, or a negative offset) is silently skipped -- out of
// v1's scope, not an error.
struct field_write_visitor_t final : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  int this_lvar_idx;
  qvector<field_write_t> writes;

  explicit field_write_visitor_t(int this_lvar_idx_)
    : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST), this_lvar_idx(this_lvar_idx_) {}

  // Matches "this + N" or "N + this" (after stripping casts), rejecting
  // anything else (another variable, multiply/array-index shapes, negative
  // literals).
  bool match_this_plus_offset(const cexpr_t *e, uint64 *out_offset) const
  {
    e = skip_casts(e);
    if ( e == nullptr || e->op != cot_add )
      return false;

    const cexpr_t *a = skip_casts(e->x);
    const cexpr_t *b = skip_casts(e->y);
    const cexpr_t *num = nullptr;
    const cexpr_t *var = nullptr;
    if ( a != nullptr && a->op == cot_var && a->v.idx == this_lvar_idx
      && b != nullptr && b->op == cot_num )
    {
      var = a;
      num = b;
    }
    else if ( b != nullptr && b->op == cot_var && b->v.idx == this_lvar_idx
      && a != nullptr && a->op == cot_num )
    {
      var = b;
      num = a;
    }
    else
    {
      return false;
    }
    (void)var;

    if ( num->is_negative_const() )
      return false; // negative offset -- out of v1 scope, just skip

    *out_offset = num->numval();
    return true;
  }

  void record_write(uint64 offset, const tinfo_t &deref_type)
  {
    int width = int(deref_type.get_size());
    if ( width <= 0 )
      return; // couldn't size the deref'd type -- nothing usable to record

    field_write_t fw;
    fw.offset = offset;
    fw.width = width;
    fw.is_pointer = deref_type.is_ptr();
    writes.push_back(fw);
  }

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op != cot_asg )
      return 0;

    cexpr_t *lhs = e->x;

    // Form 1: *(TYPE*)(this + N) = expr; -- also covers the offset-0 case,
    // where Hex-Rays renders the address as bare "this" with no "+ 0" cot_add
    // node at all (verified against a real ctor: "*(_QWORD *)a1 = &vtable;"
    // has no add node, unlike every other offset in the same function).
    if ( lhs->op == cot_ptr )
    {
      const cexpr_t *inner = skip_casts(lhs->x);
      if ( inner != nullptr && inner->op == cot_var && inner->v.idx == this_lvar_idx )
      {
        record_write(0, lhs->type);
      }
      else
      {
        uint64 offset;
        if ( match_this_plus_offset(lhs->x, &offset) )
          record_write(offset, lhs->type);
      }
      return 0;
    }

    // Form 2: already-member-accessed this->field = expr; (Hex-Rays
    // sometimes synthesizes a partial struct access instead of leaving raw
    // pointer arithmetic -- accept either shape).
    if ( lhs->op == cot_memptr || lhs->op == cot_memref )
    {
      const cexpr_t *obj = skip_casts(lhs->x);
      if ( obj != nullptr && obj->op == cot_var && obj->v.idx == this_lvar_idx )
        record_write(lhs->m, lhs->type);
      return 0;
    }

    return 0;
  }
};

// Looks for the first "operator new"/"operator new[]" call site, with a
// constant size argument, among ctor_ea's direct callers' decompiled
// bodies. This is a small, focused re-derivation of the "find the
// allocation size" idea -- NOT a reuse of the existing singleton-chain
// feature's resolve_allocator_chain(), which is scoped to finding a global
// store, not a `new` call's size argument. Tolerates not finding one.
struct operator_new_size_visitor_t final : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  bool found = false;
  uint64 found_size = 0;

  operator_new_size_visitor_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  static bool is_operator_new_callee(ea_t callee_ea)
  {
    qstring name;
    if ( get_short_name(&name, callee_ea) <= 0 )
      return false;
    return name.find("operator new") != qstring::npos;
  }

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( found )
      return 1; // already have an answer, stop walking

    if ( e->op != cot_call )
      return 0;

    const cexpr_t *callee = skip_casts(e->x);
    if ( callee == nullptr || callee->op != cot_obj )
      return 0;
    if ( !is_operator_new_callee(callee->obj_ea) )
      return 0;

    if ( e->a == nullptr || e->a->empty() )
      return 0;

    const cexpr_t *arg0 = skip_casts(&(*e->a)[0]);
    if ( arg0 == nullptr || arg0->op != cot_num )
      return 0;

    found_size = arg0->numval();
    found = true;
    return 1;
  }
};

// Tries to find a constant `operator new(SIZE)` call among ctor_ea's direct
// callers. Returns false (no *out_size written) if none is found -- this is
// tolerated, not an error.
bool find_allocation_size(ea_t ctor_ea, uint64 *out_size)
{
  qvector<ea_t> callers = find_callers_of(ctor_ea);
  for ( ea_t caller_ea : callers )
  {
    func_t *pfn = get_func(caller_ea);
    if ( pfn == nullptr )
      continue;

    hexrays_failure_t hf;
    cfuncptr_t cf = decompile_func(pfn, &hf);
    if ( cf == nullptr )
      continue;

    operator_new_size_visitor_t visitor;
    visitor.apply_to(&cf->body, nullptr);
    if ( visitor.found )
    {
      *out_size = visitor.found_size;
      return true;
    }
  }
  return false;
}

// One member slated for the synthesized struct, in final offset order.
struct planned_member_t
{
  uint64 offset = 0;
  int width = 0;
  bool is_pointer = false;
  bool is_vfptr = false;
  bool is_gap = false;
};

qstring sanitize_type_name(const qstring &class_name)
{
  qstring name = SYNTHETIC_PREFIX;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < class_name.length(); ++i )
  {
    char c = class_name[i];
    bool ok = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z')
           || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';
    name.append(ok ? c : '_');
  }
  return name;
}

// Builds a tinfo_t for a real (non-gap, non-vfptr) field from its recorded
// width/is_pointer, or returns false (unsupported width -- out of v1 scope)
// if width isn't one of {1, 2, 4, 8}.
bool build_field_type(int width, bool is_pointer, tinfo_t *out)
{
  if ( width == 8 && is_pointer )
  {
    tinfo_t voidt;
    if ( !voidt.create_simple_type(BTF_VOID) )
      return false;
    return out->create_ptr(voidt);
  }

  type_t base;
  switch ( width )
  {
    case 1: base = BTF_UINT8;  break;
    case 2: base = BTF_UINT16; break;
    case 4: base = BTF_UINT32; break;
    case 8: base = BTF_UINT64; break;
    default: return false;
  }
  return out->create_simple_type(base);
}

bool build_gap_type(uint64 gap_size, tinfo_t *out)
{
  tinfo_t bytet;
  if ( !bytet.create_simple_type(BTF_UINT8) )
    return false;
  return out->create_array(bytet, uint32(gap_size));
}

} // namespace

layout_synth_result_t synthesize_struct_layout(
    ea_t ctor_ea,
    const qstring &class_name,
    ea_t vft_ea,
    bool is_polymorphic)
{
  layout_synth_result_t result;
  result.class_name = class_name;
  result.ctor_ea = ctor_ea;

  func_t *pfn = get_func(ctor_ea);
  if ( pfn == nullptr )
  {
    result.reason = "constructor candidate has no containing function";
    return result;
  }

  hexrays_failure_t hf;
  cfuncptr_t cf = decompile_func(pfn, &hf);
  if ( cf == nullptr )
  {
    result.reason = "decompilation of constructor candidate failed";
    return result;
  }

  // Same technique used elsewhere in this plugin (e.g.
  // find_self_registered_global in ctree_analysis.cpp): the ctor's "this"
  // is its first is_arg_var() lvar.
  lvars_t *lvars = cf->get_lvars();
  int this_lvar_idx = -1;
  if ( lvars != nullptr )
  {
    for ( size_t i = 0; i < lvars->size(); ++i )
    {
      if ( (*lvars)[i].is_arg_var() )
      {
        this_lvar_idx = int(i);
        break;
      }
    }
  }

  if ( this_lvar_idx < 0 )
  {
    result.reason = "constructor has no argument lvars (no 'this' parameter found)";
    return result;
  }

  field_write_visitor_t visitor(this_lvar_idx);
  visitor.apply_to(&cf->body, nullptr);

  if ( visitor.writes.empty() )
  {
    result.reason = "no direct field-initialization writes found on constructor's 'this' parameter";
    return result;
  }

  // Dedupe exact duplicates (same offset+width+is_pointer -- e.g. written
  // twice), then sort by offset for the overlap/gap pass.
  qvector<field_write_t> &writes = visitor.writes;
  std::sort(
      writes.begin(), writes.end(),
      [](const field_write_t &a, const field_write_t &b)
      {
        if ( a.offset != b.offset )
          return a.offset < b.offset;
        return a.width < b.width;
      });

  qvector<field_write_t> deduped;
  for ( const field_write_t &fw : writes )
  {
    if ( !deduped.empty()
      && deduped.back().offset == fw.offset
      && deduped.back().width == fw.width
      && deduped.back().is_pointer == fw.is_pointer )
    {
      continue; // exact duplicate write, drop it
    }
    deduped.push_back(fw);
  }

  // Overlap detection: any two recorded (differently-shaped or not) writes
  // whose [offset, offset+width) ranges intersect means we can't trust the
  // layout -- drop the whole class rather than guess.
  uint64 running_end = 0;
  bool first = true;
  for ( const field_write_t &fw : deduped )
  {
    if ( !first && fw.offset < running_end )
    {
      result.reason.sprnt(
          "overlapping/conflicting field writes at offset 0x%llx",
          (unsigned long long)fw.offset);
      return result;
    }
    running_end = std::max<uint64>(running_end, fw.offset + fw.width);
    first = false;
  }

  // Validate widths up front -- unsupported widths take the whole class out
  // of v1's scope (rather than silently dropping just that one field).
  for ( const field_write_t &fw : deduped )
  {
    if ( fw.width != 1 && fw.width != 2 && fw.width != 4 && fw.width != 8 )
    {
      result.reason.sprnt(
          "unsupported field width %d at offset 0x%llx",
          fw.width,
          (unsigned long long)fw.offset);
      return result;
    }
  }

  qvector<planned_member_t> planned;
  bool offset0_covered = !deduped.empty() && deduped[0].offset == 0;

  if ( is_polymorphic && !offset0_covered )
  {
    planned_member_t vfptr;
    vfptr.offset = 0;
    vfptr.width = 8;
    vfptr.is_pointer = true;
    vfptr.is_vfptr = true;
    planned.push_back(vfptr);
  }

  for ( const field_write_t &fw : deduped )
  {
    planned_member_t m;
    m.offset = fw.offset;
    m.width = fw.width;
    m.is_pointer = fw.is_pointer;
    planned.push_back(m);
  }

  std::sort(
      planned.begin(), planned.end(),
      [](const planned_member_t &a, const planned_member_t &b)
      {
        return a.offset < b.offset;
      });

  // Total object size: an operator new(SIZE) call among the ctor's direct
  // callers, if one is found, tells us the true end of the object so we can
  // add one final trailing padding member. Not finding one is tolerated --
  // we just size the struct to the last recorded field's end offset.
  uint64 alloc_size = 0;
  bool have_alloc_size = find_allocation_size(ctor_ea, &alloc_size);

  udt_type_data_t udt;
  udt.is_union = false;

  uint64 next_offset = 0;
  int gap_index = 0;
  for ( const planned_member_t &m : planned )
  {
    if ( m.offset > next_offset )
    {
      uint64 gap_size = m.offset - next_offset;
      tinfo_t gap_type;
      if ( !build_gap_type(gap_size, &gap_type) )
      {
        result.reason.sprnt("failed to build padding type for gap at offset 0x%llx", (unsigned long long)next_offset);
        return result;
      }
      qstring gap_name;
      gap_name.sprnt("gap_0x%llx", (unsigned long long)next_offset);
      udt.add_member(gap_name.c_str(), gap_type, next_offset * 8);
      ++gap_index;
    }

    tinfo_t field_type;
    if ( m.is_vfptr )
    {
      if ( !field_type.create_ptr(tinfo_t(BTF_VOID)) )
      {
        result.reason = "failed to build vfptr member type";
        return result;
      }
      udt.add_member("vfptr", field_type, m.offset * 8);
    }
    else
    {
      if ( !build_field_type(m.width, m.is_pointer, &field_type) )
      {
        result.reason.sprnt("failed to build field type for offset 0x%llx", (unsigned long long)m.offset);
        return result;
      }
      qstring field_name;
      field_name.sprnt("field_0x%llx", (unsigned long long)m.offset);
      udt.add_member(field_name.c_str(), field_type, m.offset * 8);
    }

    next_offset = m.offset + m.width;
  }

  if ( have_alloc_size && alloc_size > next_offset )
  {
    uint64 gap_size = alloc_size - next_offset;
    tinfo_t gap_type;
    if ( build_gap_type(gap_size, &gap_type) )
    {
      qstring gap_name;
      gap_name.sprnt("gap_0x%llx", (unsigned long long)next_offset);
      udt.add_member(gap_name.c_str(), gap_type, next_offset * 8);
      next_offset = alloc_size;
    }
    // If building the trailing pad type somehow fails, just leave the
    // struct sized to the last recorded field -- no guessing further.
  }

  tinfo_t struct_type;
  if ( !struct_type.create_udt(udt) )
  {
    result.reason = "tinfo_t::create_udt failed";
    return result;
  }

  qstring type_name = sanitize_type_name(class_name);
  if ( struct_type.set_named_type(nullptr, type_name.c_str(), NTF_TYPE | NTF_REPLACE) != TERR_OK )
  {
    result.reason = "tinfo_t::set_named_type failed";
    return result;
  }

  result.type_name = type_name;
  result.resolved = true;
  return result;
}

```

`sm-global-finder/src/struct_synth.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>
#include <idp.hpp>

// Result of attempting to synthesize a best-effort struct layout for one
// class from its constructor's direct field-initialization writes on
// "this". This is a conservative, read-only-application v1: on success it
// only registers a new Local Type and leaves a comment on the constructor
// pointing at it -- it never retypes any lvar/parameter/global (see
// struct_synth.cpp for the full rationale).
struct layout_synth_result_t
{
  qstring class_name;
  ea_t ctor_ea = BADADDR;
  qstring type_name;  // Local Types name, populated when resolved == true
  bool resolved = false;
  qstring reason;     // populated when resolved == false
};

// Decompiles ctor_ea and walks its body for "*(TYPE*)(this + N) = expr;"
// (including the bare "*(TYPE*)this = expr;" shape Hex-Rays uses for offset
// 0, with no explicit "+ 0" node -- verified against a real ctor) /
// already-member-accessed "this->field = expr;" style writes on the
// constructor's own "this" parameter (its first is_arg_var() lvar), and --
// provided the recorded field writes don't conflict with each other --
// synthesizes a best-effort struct type describing the class's memory
// layout. The type is registered into Local Types (via
// tinfo_t::set_named_type) under a "__sm_layout_<ClassName>" name (any
// "::" in class_name replaced with "_"), and a comment is left on the
// constructor pointing at it.
//
// is_polymorphic should be true whenever vft_ea denotes a real RTTI vtable
// resolved for this class -- this plugin only ever calls this function for
// such classes (every caller loops over find_rtti_vtables() results), but
// the flag is threaded through explicitly rather than assumed, both for
// clarity at the call site and so this function stays reusable if a future
// caller ever wants to try it against a non-RTTI class. When true and
// offset 0 isn't otherwise covered by a recorded field write, an explicit
// "vfptr" (void *) member is inserted at offset 0 -- a known fact, not a
// guess, since find_rtti_vtables() already proved a vtable pointer lives
// there.
layout_synth_result_t synthesize_struct_layout(
    ea_t ctor_ea,
    const qstring &class_name,
    ea_t vft_ea,
    bool is_polymorphic);

```

cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.27)

project(SMLuaFuncFinder LANGUAGES CXX)

set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 17)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)
set(CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS OFF)

if(NOT DEFINED ENV{IDASDK} OR "$ENV{IDASDK}" STREQUAL "")
  message(FATAL_ERROR "Set IDASDK to the IDA SDK root before configuring.")
endif()

include("$ENV{IDASDK}/src/cmake/bootstrap.cmake")
find_package(idasdk REQUIRED)

ida_add_plugin(sm_lua_func_finder
  SOURCES
    src/lua_reg_array.cpp
    src/reg_scan.cpp
    src/naming.cpp
    src/lua_api_docs_lookup.cpp
    src/lua_api_docs.gen.cpp
    src/lua_argcount_collapse.cpp
    src/plugin_main.cpp
  METADATA_JSON
    ida-plugin.json
)

if(WIN32)
  set(IDA_INSTALL_DIR "" CACHE PATH "IDA installation directory for plugin deployment")
  set(SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR "${IDA_INSTALL_DIR}")
  if("${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR}" STREQUAL "")
    file(GLOB SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES
      LIST_DIRECTORIES true
      "C:/Program Files/IDA Professional *"
      "C:/Program Files/IDA Pro *"
      "C:/Program Files/IDA *")
    list(SORT SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES
      COMPARE NATURAL
      ORDER DESCENDING)
    list(LENGTH SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATE_COUNT)
    if(SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATE_COUNT GREATER 0)
      list(GET SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_CANDIDATES 0 SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR)
    endif()
  endif()

  if(NOT "${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR}" STREQUAL "")
    set(SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR
      "${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_INSTALL_DIR}/plugins/sm_lua_func_finder")

    add_custom_command(TARGET sm_lua_func_finder POST_BUILD
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E make_directory
        "${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}"
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E copy_if_different
        "$<TARGET_FILE:sm_lua_func_finder>"
        "${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}/$<TARGET_FILE_NAME:sm_lua_func_finder>"
      COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" -E copy_if_different
        "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/ida-plugin.json"
        "${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}/ida-plugin.json"
      COMMENT "Installing SM Lua Function Finder in ${SM_LUA_FUNC_FINDER_PLUGIN_DIR}"
    )
  else()
    message(WARNING "No IDA installation found; skipping post-build plugin deployment.")
  endif()
endif()

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/README.md`:

```md
# SM-Lua-Func-Finder

Discovers Lua-callable C functions in Scrap Mechanic's `ScrapMechanic.exe`
(and similar Lua-51-embedding binaries) by scanning for `luaL_register()`
call sites, renames the discovered functions `Lua_<Owner>_<func>`, and
collapses the argument-count-check boilerplate that opens nearly every one
of them so the real function body reads cleanly.

### NOTE: This project is fully AI generated, I don't take any credit for anything in here.

## What it does

### 1. Scan and rename (`Edit -> Plugins -> SM Lua Function Finder`)

- Finds every function that calls `luaL_register()` (`sm.*` static
  namespaces) or precedes it with a matching `luaL_newmetatable()`
  (userdata methods), walking the referenced `luaL_Reg` array to recover
  each registered Lua name.
- Renames the underlying C function `Lua_<Owner>_<func>` (e.g.
  `Lua_sm_audio_play`, `Lua_Tool_Character_getTpBonePos`), merging labels
  when the same implementation is shared across multiple owners.
- Adds a doc comment pulled from the `sm_api_differ` JSON dump where an
  entry matches, and shows every result in a modal chooser plus a written
  log next to the current database
  (`<idb dir>\sm_lua_func_finder_scan.log`).

### 2. Argument-count-check collapse (automatic, every decompile)

Nearly every Lua-bound C function in this binary opens with boilerplate
that checks `lua_gettop(L)` against the expected argument count and calls
`luaL_error(L, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", ...)` on mismatch. Since
`luaL_error()` never returns (it longjmps out of the Lua VM), Hex-Rays still
has to show the "got count" recompute, the error call, and (in one of the
two equivalent shapes) an unreachable early return -- pure noise around the
one thing that actually matters, the expected count. Both shapes:

```c
if ( (unsigned int)lua_gettop(a1) != 1 )
{
  v2 = lua_gettop(a1);
  luaL_error(a1, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", 1, v2);
  return 0;
}
... real function body ...
```

```c
if ( (unsigned int)lua_gettop(a1) == 1 )
{
  ... real function body ...
}
else
{
  v2 = lua_gettop(a1);
  luaL_error(a1, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", 1, v2);
}
```

are collapsed into a single synthesized call (real function body
untouched, in place):

```c
L_checkArgCount(a1, 1);
... real function body ...
```

This runs automatically on every decompile, right before pseudocode text
is generated (at `hxe_maturity`/`CMAT_FINAL`) -- no user interaction
required.

## Requirements

- IDA Professional 9.2 or newer
- Hex-Rays decompiler
- IDA SDK configured through the `IDASDK` environment variable (a checkout of
  [HexRaysSA/ida-sdk](https://github.com/HexRaysSA/ida-sdk) at a tag with
  CMake support, e.g. `v9.3.1-release` -- not the classic hex-rays SDK zip)
- CMake 3.27 or newer
- Visual Studio with C++ build tools on Windows

## Build

```powershell
$env:IDASDK = "C:\path\to\ida-sdk"
cmake -S . -B build -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64
cmake --build build --config Release
```

On Windows, the build installs the DLL and metadata into IDA's plugin
directory automatically if it has write access; otherwise copy
`sm_lua_func_finder.dll` and `ida-plugin.json` into
`<IDA install>\plugins\sm_lua_func_finder\` yourself (this typically needs
an elevated copy, since `Program Files` is admin-writable only).

## Known limitations (v1)

- The rename pass only recognizes registrations reachable through
  `luaL_register()`/`luaL_newmetatable()` call sites that Hex-Rays can
  decompile; unusual registration shapes are skipped and logged.
- The argcount-check collapse only recognizes the two boilerplate shapes
  shown above, anchored on the exact `"Expected %d arguments (got %d)"`
  format string; other error-message wording or argument-count-check
  styles are left untouched.

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/gen_api_docs.py`:

```py
import json, glob, os

SRC = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "sm_api", "json")
OUT = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "lua_api_docs.gen.cpp")

def esc(s):
    return s.replace("\\", "\\\\").replace("\"", "\\\"").replace("\n", "\\n").replace("\r", "")

def first_text(doc):
    if not doc:
        return ""
    for t in doc.get("text") or []:
        t = t.strip()
        if t and not t.startswith("@"):
            return t
    return ""

def param_sig(doc):
    parts = []
    for p in (doc or {}).get("params") or []:
        if len(p) >= 2:
            parts.append(f"{p[0]} {p[1]}")
    return ", ".join(parts)

# owner_kind: 0 = namespace (dotted, e.g. "sm.audio"), 1 = userdata (e.g. "Character")
entries = []  # (owner_kind, owner, funcname, params, summary)
seen = set()

for path in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(SRC, "namespace_Game_*.json"))):
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        data = json.load(f)
    ns = data.get("namespace", "")
    for fn in data.get("functions", []) or []:
        name = fn.get("name", "")
        if not name:
            continue
        key = (0, ns, name)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        doc = fn.get("doc") or {}
        entries.append((0, ns, name, param_sig(doc), first_text(doc)))

for path in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(SRC, "userdata_Game_*.json"))):
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        data = json.load(f)
    ud = data.get("userdata", "")
    for fn in data.get("functions", []) or []:
        name = fn.get("name", "")
        if not name:
            continue
        key = (1, ud, name)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        doc = fn.get("doc") or {}
        entries.append((1, ud, name, param_sig(doc), first_text(doc)))

entries.sort(key=lambda e: (e[0], e[1], e[2]))

with open(OUT, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    f.write("// Generated from sm_api_differ json_ch1 dump (Game app: namespace_Game_*.json,\n")
    f.write("// userdata_Game_*.json). Do not edit by hand -- see gen_api_docs.py.\n")
    f.write("#include \"lua_api_docs.h\"\n\n")
    f.write("const lua_api_doc_entry_t g_lua_api_docs[] =\n{\n")
    for kind, owner, name, params, summary in entries:
        f.write(
            f'  {{ {kind}, "{esc(owner)}", "{esc(name)}", "{esc(params)}", "{esc(summary)}" }},\n'
        )
    f.write("};\n\n")
    f.write("const size_t g_lua_api_docs_count = sizeof(g_lua_api_docs) / sizeof(g_lua_api_docs[0]);\n")

print(f"Wrote {len(entries)} entries to {OUT}")

```
