```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/lua_api_docs.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <cstddef>

// One documented function pulled from the sm_api_differ json_ch1 dump
// (see gen_api_docs.py). owner_kind distinguishes a dotted static namespace
// ("sm.audio") from a userdata type name ("Character").
struct lua_api_doc_entry_t
{
  int owner_kind;        // 0 = namespace, 1 = userdata
  const char *owner;
  const char *funcname;
  const char *params;    // "type name, type name, ..." (already formatted)
  const char *summary;    // first non-@tag doc line
};

extern const lua_api_doc_entry_t g_lua_api_docs[];
extern const size_t g_lua_api_docs_count;

// Finds the doc entry for (is_userdata, owner, funcname), or nullptr if the
// sm_api_differ dump doesn't cover it (e.g. it wasn't documented yet).
const lua_api_doc_entry_t *find_lua_api_doc(
    bool is_userdata,
    const char *owner,
    const char *funcname);

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/lua_api_docs_lookup.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "lua_api_docs.h"

#include <cstring>

const lua_api_doc_entry_t *find_lua_api_doc(
    bool is_userdata,
    const char *owner,
    const char *funcname)
{
  int want_kind = is_userdata ? 1 : 0;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < g_lua_api_docs_count; ++i )
  {
    const lua_api_doc_entry_t &e = g_lua_api_docs[i];
    if ( e.owner_kind == want_kind
      && std::strcmp(e.owner, owner) == 0
      && std::strcmp(e.funcname, funcname) == 0 )
    {
      return &e;
    }
  }
  return nullptr;
}

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/lua_argcount_collapse.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "lua_argcount_collapse.h"

#include <name.hpp>
#include <bytes.hpp>
#include <typeinf.hpp>

#include <cstring>
#include <map>
#include <set>

namespace
{

constexpr char EXPECTED_ARGS_FORMAT[] = "Expected %d arguments (got %d)";

const cexpr_t *strip_casts(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  while ( e != nullptr && e->op == cot_cast )
    e = e->x;
  return e;
}

// Same string-content resolution convention as reg_scan.cpp's
// resolve_str_arg(): either a literal folded directly into the call, or a
// global pointer-to-string read by value.
bool resolve_str_arg(const cexpr_t *e, qstring *out)
{
  e = strip_casts(e);
  if ( e == nullptr )
    return false;

  if ( e->op == cot_str )
  {
    if ( e->string == nullptr )
      return false;
    *out = e->string;
    return true;
  }

  if ( e->op == cot_obj )
  {
    ea_t g = e->obj_ea;
    if ( !is_loaded(g) )
      return false;
    ea_t p = ea_t(get_qword(g));
    if ( p == 0 || !is_loaded(p) )
      return false;
    return get_strlit_contents(out, p, -1, STRTYPE_C) > 0;
  }

  return false;
}

bool is_expected_args_format_string(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  qstring contents;
  return resolve_str_arg(e, &contents) && contents == EXPECTED_ARGS_FORMAT;
}

// Lazily resolves a named import's ea once per process, caching the result
// (the database doesn't change which imports exist mid-session).
ea_t named_ea(const char *name)
{
  static std::map<qstring, ea_t> cache;
  qstring key = name;
  auto it = cache.find(key);
  if ( it != cache.end() )
    return it->second;
  ea_t ea = get_name_ea(BADADDR, name);
  cache[key] = ea;
  return ea;
}

// True if `e` (after stripping casts) is a `cot_call` whose callee resolves
// (via cot_obj/obj_ea) to the named imported function. On success, *args is
// the call's argument list.
bool is_call_to_named(const cexpr_t *e, const char *name, const carglist_t **args)
{
  e = strip_casts(e);
  if ( e == nullptr || e->op != cot_call || e->a == nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *callee = strip_casts(e->x);
  if ( callee == nullptr || callee->op != cot_obj )
    return false;

  ea_t target_ea = named_ea(name);
  if ( target_ea == BADADDR || callee->obj_ea != target_ea )
    return false;

  *args = e->a;
  return true;
}

bool same_expr(const cexpr_t *a, const cexpr_t *b)
{
  a = strip_casts(a);
  b = strip_casts(b);
  if ( a == nullptr || b == nullptr )
    return false;
  return a->equal_effect(*b);
}

// True if `cond` (after stripping casts) is `(cast) lua_gettop(L) <op> N`
// or `N <op> (cast) lua_gettop(L)`, for the requested comparison opcode.
// On success, *l_expr is the `L` argument expression (still inside the
// live ctree -- not cloned) and *n_expr is the literal `N` expression.
bool match_gettop_compare(const cexpr_t *cond, ctype_t want_op, const cexpr_t **l_expr, const cexpr_t **n_expr)
{
  cond = strip_casts(cond);
  if ( cond == nullptr || cond->op != want_op )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *x = strip_casts(cond->x);
  const cexpr_t *y = strip_casts(cond->y);

  const cexpr_t *call_side = nullptr;
  const cexpr_t *num_side = nullptr;
  if ( y != nullptr && y->op == cot_num )
  {
    call_side = x;
    num_side = y;
  }
  else if ( x != nullptr && x->op == cot_num )
  {
    call_side = y;
    num_side = x;
  }
  else
  {
    return false;
  }

  const carglist_t *args = nullptr;
  if ( !is_call_to_named(call_side, "lua_gettop", &args) || args->size() < 1 )
    return false;

  *l_expr = &args->at(0);
  *n_expr = num_side;
  return true;
}

// True if `stmt` is `<var> = lua_gettop(l_expr);` (the throwaway "got count"
// recompute) -- matched structurally against `l_expr` via same_expr(), not
// by variable name.
bool match_gettop_recompute(const cinsn_t *stmt, const cexpr_t *l_expr)
{
  if ( stmt == nullptr || stmt->op != cit_expr || stmt->cexpr == nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *asg = strip_casts(stmt->cexpr);
  if ( asg == nullptr || asg->op != cot_asg )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *lhs = strip_casts(asg->x);
  if ( lhs == nullptr || lhs->op != cot_var )
    return false;

  const carglist_t *args = nullptr;
  if ( !is_call_to_named(asg->y, "lua_gettop", &args) || args->size() < 1 )
    return false;

  return same_expr(&args->at(0), l_expr);
}

// True if `stmt` is the `luaL_error(L, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", N,
// <got-count expr>);` call, for the SAME `l_expr` (structural equality).
// On success, *n_expr is the expected-count literal (2nd arg to
// luaL_error -- the 3rd call argument overall).
bool match_luaL_error_call(const cinsn_t *stmt, const cexpr_t *l_expr, const cexpr_t **n_expr)
{
  if ( stmt == nullptr || stmt->op != cit_expr || stmt->cexpr == nullptr )
    return false;

  const carglist_t *args = nullptr;
  if ( !is_call_to_named(stmt->cexpr, "luaL_error", &args) || args->size() < 4 )
    return false;

  if ( !same_expr(&args->at(0), l_expr) )
    return false;

  if ( !is_expected_args_format_string(&args->at(1)) )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *n_lit = strip_casts(&args->at(2));
  if ( n_lit == nullptr || n_lit->op != cot_num )
    return false;

  *n_expr = n_lit;
  return true;
}

struct argcount_match_t
{
  cinsn_t *matched_stmt = nullptr;  // the whole outer cit_if
  const cexpr_t *l_expr = nullptr;  // lua_State* expression (live, uncloned)
  const cexpr_t *n_expr = nullptr;  // expected-argcount literal (live, uncloned)
  bool has_else = false;            // Shape B: real body must be reparented
  cinsn_t *real_body = nullptr;     // Shape B only: the ithen block to keep
};

// Shape A: early-return-on-mismatch.
//   if ( (unsigned int)lua_gettop(L) != N )
//   {
//     v2 = lua_gettop(L);
//     luaL_error(L, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", N, v2);
//     return ...;
//   }
//   ... real body follows as later siblings, untouched ...
bool try_match_shape_a(cinsn_t *outer_if, argcount_match_t *out)
{
  if ( outer_if == nullptr || outer_if->op != cit_if || outer_if->cif == nullptr )
    return false;
  if ( outer_if->cif->ielse != nullptr )
    return false; // shape A has no else -- that's shape B

  const cexpr_t *l_expr = nullptr;
  const cexpr_t *n_expr = nullptr;
  if ( !match_gettop_compare(&outer_if->cif->expr, cot_ne, &l_expr, &n_expr) )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *body = outer_if->cif->ithen;
  if ( body == nullptr || body->op != cit_block || body->cblock == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *recompute_stmt = nullptr;
  cinsn_t *error_stmt = nullptr;
  cinsn_t *return_stmt = nullptr;
  size_t stmt_count = 0;
  for ( cinsn_t &stmt : *body->cblock )
  {
    ++stmt_count;
    if ( stmt.op == cit_return )
      return_stmt = &stmt;
    else if ( stmt.op == cit_expr )
    {
      // Disambiguate the two cit_expr statements by trying the more
      // specific match (luaL_error) first.
      const cexpr_t *n_from_error = nullptr;
      if ( error_stmt == nullptr && match_luaL_error_call(&stmt, l_expr, &n_from_error) )
        error_stmt = &stmt;
      else if ( recompute_stmt == nullptr && match_gettop_recompute(&stmt, l_expr) )
        recompute_stmt = &stmt;
    }
  }

  if ( stmt_count != 3 || recompute_stmt == nullptr || error_stmt == nullptr || return_stmt == nullptr )
    return false;

  out->matched_stmt = outer_if;
  out->l_expr = l_expr;
  out->n_expr = n_expr;
  out->has_else = false;
  out->real_body = nullptr;
  return true;
}

// Shape B: match-wraps-the-body, mismatch in the else.
//   if ( (unsigned int)lua_gettop(L) == N )
//   {
//     ... real body ...
//   }
//   else
//   {
//     v2 = lua_gettop(L);
//     luaL_error(L, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", N, v2);
//   }
bool try_match_shape_b(cinsn_t *outer_if, argcount_match_t *out)
{
  if ( outer_if == nullptr || outer_if->op != cit_if || outer_if->cif == nullptr )
    return false;
  if ( outer_if->cif->ielse == nullptr )
    return false; // shape B requires an else -- that's shape A

  const cexpr_t *l_expr = nullptr;
  const cexpr_t *n_expr = nullptr;
  if ( !match_gettop_compare(&outer_if->cif->expr, cot_eq, &l_expr, &n_expr) )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *else_body = outer_if->cif->ielse;
  if ( else_body == nullptr || else_body->op != cit_block || else_body->cblock == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *recompute_stmt = nullptr;
  cinsn_t *error_stmt = nullptr;
  size_t stmt_count = 0;
  for ( cinsn_t &stmt : *else_body->cblock )
  {
    ++stmt_count;
    if ( stmt.op != cit_expr )
      continue;
    const cexpr_t *n_from_error = nullptr;
    if ( error_stmt == nullptr && match_luaL_error_call(&stmt, l_expr, &n_from_error) )
      error_stmt = &stmt;
    else if ( recompute_stmt == nullptr && match_gettop_recompute(&stmt, l_expr) )
      recompute_stmt = &stmt;
  }

  if ( stmt_count != 2 || recompute_stmt == nullptr || error_stmt == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *body = outer_if->cif->ithen;
  if ( body == nullptr )
    return false;

  out->matched_stmt = outer_if;
  out->l_expr = l_expr;
  out->n_expr = n_expr;
  out->has_else = true;
  out->real_body = body;
  return true;
}

// Builds `L_checkArgCount(L, N)` as a synthetic call_helper expression.
// luaL_error() never returns (it longjmps out of the Lua VM via
// lua_error()/lj_err_throw()-style unwinding), so the recompute-for-the-
// message local, the luaL_error() call itself, and (in Shape A) the early
// return are all pure noise that only exist because Hex-Rays can't know
// the callee is noreturn. Collapsing them to a single named helper call
// (the same "call_helper()/vcall_helper() only ever print name(args...)"
// technique used by stl_shared_ptr_release.cpp/stl_string_release.cpp)
// keeps the check visible -- and its operands (L, N) still real, cloned
// cexpr_t nodes, so they stay clickable/renameable in the pseudocode --
// while dropping everything that exists solely to satisfy a control-flow
// edge Hex-Rays believes it needs but which can never actually be taken.
cexpr_t *make_check_call(const cexpr_t *l_expr, const cexpr_t *n_expr)
{
  tinfo_t void_type;
  if ( !void_type.create_simple_type(BTF_VOID) )
    return nullptr;

  carglist_t *args = new carglist_t();
  args->resize(2);
  args->at(0).consume_cexpr(new cexpr_t(*l_expr));
  args->at(1).consume_cexpr(new cexpr_t(*n_expr));

  return call_helper(void_type, args, "L_checkArgCount");
}

bool synthesize_and_replace_shape_a(const argcount_match_t &match)
{
  cexpr_t *call = make_check_call(match.l_expr, match.n_expr);
  if ( call == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *new_stmt = new cinsn_t();
  new_stmt->op = cit_expr;
  new_stmt->cexpr = call;
  new_stmt->ea = match.matched_stmt->ea;

  match.matched_stmt->replace_by(new_stmt);
  return true;
}

bool synthesize_and_replace_shape_b(argcount_match_t &match)
{
  cexpr_t *call = make_check_call(match.l_expr, match.n_expr);
  if ( call == nullptr )
    return false;

  // outer_if->cif owns `real_body` (the ithen subtree). replace_by() below
  // deletes the whole matched cit_if node (including its cif_t, which
  // deletes ithen/ielse in its destructor), so we must detach `real_body`
  // from the cif_t BEFORE replacing, or the reparented subtree would be
  // double-freed once as part of the old node's cleanup and again when the
  // new block housing it is eventually torn down.
  cinsn_t *real_body = match.real_body;
  match.matched_stmt->cif->ithen = nullptr;

  cblock_t *new_cblock = new cblock_t();

  // First statement: the synthesized check.
  cinsn_t check_stmt;
  check_stmt.op = cit_expr;
  check_stmt.cexpr = call;
  check_stmt.ea = match.matched_stmt->ea;
  new_cblock->push_back().swap(check_stmt);

  // Second statement: the real body, MOVED (not cloned) via cinsn_t::swap
  // -- cinsn_t's copy-assignment (used by qlist::push_back(const T&)) does
  // a deep structural copy, which would be wasteful (and needless copy
  // risk) for a whole function body; swap() just exchanges the union
  // pointers/op tags, so ownership transfers cheaply and exactly.
  new_cblock->push_back().swap(*real_body);
  delete real_body; // now cit_empty (its contents were swapped out) -- safe

  cinsn_t *new_stmt = new cinsn_t();
  new_stmt->op = cit_block;
  new_stmt->cblock = new_cblock;
  new_stmt->ea = match.matched_stmt->ea;

  match.matched_stmt->replace_by(new_stmt);
  return true;
}

struct argcount_collapser_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  int collapsed_count = 0;

  argcount_collapser_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  int idaapi visit_insn(cinsn_t *insn) override
  {
    if ( insn->op != cit_if )
      return 0;

    argcount_match_t match;
    if ( try_match_shape_a(insn, &match) )
    {
      if ( synthesize_and_replace_shape_a(match) )
        ++collapsed_count;
      return 0;
    }

    if ( try_match_shape_b(insn, &match) )
    {
      if ( synthesize_and_replace_shape_b(match) )
        ++collapsed_count;
      return 0;
    }

    return 0;
  }
};

struct used_var_collector_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  std::set<int> used_indices;

  used_var_collector_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op == cot_var )
      used_indices.insert(e->v.idx);
    return 0;
  }
};

// See stl_shared_ptr_release.cpp's hide_orphaned_locals() for the
// rationale: replace_by deletes statements but leaves now-orphaned locals
// (here, the "got count" recompute local, e.g. "v2") declared-but-unused,
// since their CVAR_USED flag isn't recalculated after our mutation. Hide
// anything no longer referenced, skipping arguments (never hide those --
// it would alter the printed function signature, not just a declaration
// line).
void hide_orphaned_locals(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  used_var_collector_t collector;
  collector.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);

  lvars_t *lvars = cfunc->get_lvars();
  if ( lvars == nullptr )
    return;

  for ( size_t i = 0; i < lvars->size(); ++i )
  {
    lvar_t &lvar = lvars->at(i);
    if ( lvar.is_arg_var() || !lvar.used() )
      continue;
    if ( collector.used_indices.find(int(i)) == collector.used_indices.end() )
      lvar.clear_used();
  }
}

} // namespace

int collapse_lua_argcount_checks(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  argcount_collapser_t collapser;
  collapser.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);
  if ( collapser.collapsed_count > 0 )
    hide_orphaned_locals(cfunc);
  return collapser.collapsed_count;
}

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/lua_argcount_collapse.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <hexrays.hpp>

// Recognizes the Lua argument-count-check boilerplate that opens nearly
// every Lua-bound C function in this binary (confirmed via the
// "Expected %d arguments (got %d)" luaL_error() format string, which has
// hundreds of xrefs -- essentially the same population this plugin's
// luaL_register() scan already discovers). Two equivalent shapes are
// recognized:
//
//   Shape A (early-return-on-mismatch):
//     if ( (unsigned int)lua_gettop(a1) != 1 )
//     {
//       v2 = lua_gettop(a1);
//       luaL_error(a1, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", 1, v2);
//       return 0;
//     }
//     ... real function body ...
//
//   Shape B (match-wraps-the-body):
//     if ( (unsigned int)lua_gettop(a1) == 1 )
//     {
//       ... real function body ...
//     }
//     else
//     {
//       v2 = lua_gettop(a1);
//       luaL_error(a1, "Expected %d arguments (got %d)", 1, v2);
//     }
//
// luaL_error() never returns (it longjmps out of the Lua VM), so everything
// besides the check itself is pure noise that only exists because Hex-Rays
// can't know that. Both shapes are collapsed into a single synthetic
// `L_checkArgCount(L, N);` statement (named to match this exact binary's
// existing L_checkType/L_getVM/L_getCharacter helper-naming convention),
// dropping the recompute-for-the-error-message local, the luaL_error() call,
// and (for Shape A) the early return -- while preserving the real function
// body verbatim.
//
// Runs at hxe_maturity/CMAT_FINAL, mutating the ctree via cinsn_t::replace_by
// before pseudocode text is generated. Returns the number of collapsed
// occurrences within `cfunc`'s body.
int collapse_lua_argcount_checks(cfunc_t *cfunc);

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/lua_reg_array.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "lua_reg_array.h"

#include <bytes.hpp>
#include <segment.hpp>

namespace
{

constexpr int MAX_REG_ENTRIES = 1024;
constexpr int ENTRY_SIZE = 16; // {const char *name; lua_CFunction func;} on x64

} // namespace

qvector<lua_reg_entry_t> parse_luaL_reg_array(ea_t arr_ea, qstring *reason)
{
  qvector<lua_reg_entry_t> out;

  if ( arr_ea == BADADDR || !is_loaded(arr_ea) )
  {
    if ( reason != nullptr )
      *reason = "array address is unmapped";
    return out;
  }

  ea_t entry_ea = arr_ea;
  for ( int i = 0; i < MAX_REG_ENTRIES; ++i, entry_ea += ENTRY_SIZE )
  {
    if ( !is_loaded(entry_ea) || !is_loaded(entry_ea + 8) )
    {
      if ( reason != nullptr && out.empty() )
        *reason = "array runs off the end of a mapped segment before a NULL terminator";
      break;
    }

    ea_t name_ptr = ea_t(get_qword(entry_ea));
    ea_t func_ptr = ea_t(get_qword(entry_ea + 8));
    if ( name_ptr == 0 && func_ptr == 0 )
      return out; // clean terminator

    if ( name_ptr == 0 || !is_loaded(name_ptr) )
    {
      if ( reason != nullptr && out.empty() )
        *reason = "first array slot's name pointer is invalid";
      break;
    }

    qstring name;
    if ( get_strlit_contents(&name, name_ptr, -1, STRTYPE_C) <= 0 || name.empty() )
    {
      if ( reason != nullptr && out.empty() )
        *reason = "first array slot's name isn't a readable C string";
      break;
    }

    lua_reg_entry_t entry;
    entry.name = name;
    entry.func_ea = func_ptr;
    out.push_back(entry);
  }

  return out;
}

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/lua_reg_array.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>

// One {const char *name; lua_CFunction func;} slot out of a luaL_Reg array.
struct lua_reg_entry_t
{
  qstring name;
  ea_t func_ea;
};

// Parses the null-terminated luaL_Reg array (16-byte {name_ptr, func_ptr}
// slots on x64) starting at arr_ea, stopping at the first {0,0} terminator.
// Returns the entries found (possibly empty); *reason is filled when the
// array couldn't be read at all (arr_ea unmapped, first slot unreadable).
qvector<lua_reg_entry_t> parse_luaL_reg_array(ea_t arr_ea, qstring *reason);

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/naming.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "naming.h"
#include "lua_api_docs.h"

#include <map>

namespace
{

// Turns an owner/funcname piece into a valid identifier fragment: "sm.audio"
// -> "sm_audio", "getTpBonePos" -> unchanged.
qstring sanitize_ident(const qstring &s)
{
  qstring out = s;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < out.length(); ++i )
  {
    char c = out[i];
    bool ok = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';
    if ( !ok )
      out[i] = '_';
  }
  return out;
}

bool has_label(const qvector<name_label_t> &labels, const qstring &owner, const qstring &funcname)
{
  for ( const name_label_t &l : labels )
  {
    if ( l.owner == owner && l.funcname == funcname )
      return true;
  }
  return false;
}

} // namespace

qvector<final_name_t> build_final_names(const qvector<lua_func_reg_t> &regs)
{
  std::map<ea_t, qvector<size_t>> groups;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < regs.size(); ++i )
    groups[regs[i].func_ea].push_back(i);

  qvector<final_name_t> out;
  out.reserve(groups.size());

  for ( const auto &kv : groups )
  {
    qvector<name_label_t> labels;
    for ( size_t idx : kv.second )
    {
      const lua_func_reg_t &r = regs[idx];
      if ( has_label(labels, r.owner, r.funcname) )
        continue;
      name_label_t label;
      label.owner = r.owner;
      label.is_userdata = r.is_userdata;
      label.funcname = r.funcname;
      labels.push_back(label);
    }

    final_name_t fin;
    fin.func_ea = kv.first;
    fin.labels = labels;

    fin.name = "Lua";
    for ( const name_label_t &l : labels )
    {
      fin.name.cat_sprnt(
          "_%s_%s",
          sanitize_ident(l.owner).c_str(),
          sanitize_ident(l.funcname).c_str());
    }

    for ( const name_label_t &l : labels )
    {
      const lua_api_doc_entry_t *doc = find_lua_api_doc(l.is_userdata, l.owner.c_str(), l.funcname.c_str());
      if ( doc == nullptr )
        continue;
      if ( !fin.comment.empty() )
        fin.comment += "\n\n";
      fin.comment.cat_sprnt(
          "%s.%s(%s)\n%s",
          l.owner.c_str(), l.funcname.c_str(),
          doc->params, doc->summary);
    }

    out.push_back(fin);
  }

  return out;
}

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/naming.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>

#include "reg_scan.h"

// A single (owner, funcname) label contributing to a renamed function --
// one function can carry several when the same implementation is shared
// across owners (e.g. a common helper registered under two userdata types).
struct name_label_t
{
  qstring owner;
  bool is_userdata;
  qstring funcname;
};

struct final_name_t
{
  ea_t func_ea;
  qstring name;         // e.g. "Lua_sm_audio_play" or "Lua_Tool_Character_getTpBonePos"
  qstring comment;      // doc summary pulled from lua_api_docs.gen.cpp, if any matched
  qvector<name_label_t> labels;
};

// Groups scan results by func_ea, merges duplicate (owner, funcname) pairs,
// and builds the final IDA name + comment for each distinct function.
qvector<final_name_t> build_final_names(const qvector<lua_func_reg_t> &regs);

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/plugin_main.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "reg_scan.h"
#include "naming.h"
#include "lua_argcount_collapse.h"

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>
#include <idp.hpp>
#include <loader.hpp>
#include <kernwin.hpp>
#include <name.hpp>
#include <funcs.hpp>
#include <hexrays.hpp>
#include <fpro.h>

namespace
{

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Results chooser
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

qstring join_labels(const qvector<name_label_t> &labels)
{
  qstring out;
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < labels.size(); ++i )
  {
    if ( i != 0 )
      out += ", ";
    out.cat_sprnt("%s.%s", labels[i].owner.c_str(), labels[i].funcname.c_str());
  }
  return out;
}

struct scan_results_popup_t final : chooser_t
{
  static const int widths[];
  static const char *const headers[];

  const qvector<final_name_t> &results;

  explicit scan_results_popup_t(const qvector<final_name_t> &results_)
    : chooser_t(
        CH_MODAL | CH_KEEP,
        4,
        widths,
        headers,
        "SM Lua Function Finder Results"),
      results(results_)
  {
  }

  size_t idaapi get_count() const override
  {
    return results.size();
  }

  ea_t idaapi get_ea(size_t n) const override
  {
    return results[n].func_ea;
  }

  void idaapi get_row(
        qstrvec_t *cols_,
        int *,
        chooser_item_attrs_t *,
        size_t n) const override
  {
    const final_name_t &r = results[n];
    qstrvec_t &cols = *cols_;
    cols[0].sprnt("%a", r.func_ea);
    cols[1] = r.name;
    cols[2] = join_labels(r.labels);
    cols[3] = r.comment.empty() ? "-" : "yes";
  }
};

const int scan_results_popup_t::widths[] =
{
  CHCOL_HEX | 16,
  50,
  50,
  6,
};

const char *const scan_results_popup_t::headers[] =
{
  "Address",
  "New Name",
  "Owner(s).Function",
  "Doc",
};

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Applying results
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

func_t *ensure_func(ea_t ea)
{
  func_t *pfn = get_func(ea);
  if ( pfn != nullptr )
    return pfn;
  if ( add_func(ea) )
    return get_func(ea);
  return nullptr;
}

// Writes one line per renamed function to a log file next to the current
// database, so results survive after the modal chooser is closed.
qstring write_scan_log(const qvector<final_name_t> &results, const qstring &scan_log)
{
  char dir[QMAXPATH];
  qdirname(dir, sizeof(dir), get_path(PATH_TYPE_IDB));

  qstring log_path;
  log_path.sprnt("%s/sm_lua_func_finder_scan.log", dir);

  FILE *fp = qfopen(log_path.c_str(), "w");
  if ( fp == nullptr )
    return qstring();

  qfprintf(fp, "=== Diagnostics from the scan pass ===\n%s\n", scan_log.c_str());
  qfprintf(fp, "=== Renamed functions ===\n");
  for ( const final_name_t &r : results )
  {
    qfprintf(
        fp,
        "%a  %-60s  %s\n",
        r.func_ea,
        r.name.c_str(),
        join_labels(r.labels).c_str());
  }

  qfclose(fp);
  return log_path;
}

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Live decompiler collapse hook
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Fires once per decompiled function, right before Hex-Rays finalizes its
// ctree into pseudocode text. Mirrors IDA-STL-Utility's stl_retype.cpp
// wiring: a single callback installed via install_hexrays_callback(),
// dispatching on the hexrays_event_t passed through the va_list.
ssize_t idaapi decompiler_callback(void *, hexrays_event_t event, va_list va)
{
  if ( event == hxe_maturity )
  {
    cfunc_t *cfunc = va_arg(va, cfunc_t *);
    const ctree_maturity_t new_maturity = ctree_maturity_t(va_arg(va, int));
    if ( new_maturity == CMAT_FINAL )
    {
      const int collapsed = collapse_lua_argcount_checks(cfunc);
      if ( collapsed > 0 )
        msg("SM Lua Function Finder: collapsed %d argcount check(s) in function=%a\n", collapsed, cfunc->entry_ea);
    }
    return 0;
  }

  return 0;
}

bool idaapi run(size_t)
{
  qstring log;
  qvector<lua_func_reg_t> regs = scan_lua_registrations(&log);
  if ( regs.empty() )
  {
    warning(
        "SM Lua Function Finder: no luaL_register()-based Lua function "
        "registrations were found in this database.\n\n%s",
        log.c_str());
    return true;
  }

  qvector<final_name_t> finals = build_final_names(regs);

  size_t renamed_count = 0;
  size_t commented_count = 0;
  for ( const final_name_t &r : finals )
  {
    func_t *pfn = ensure_func(r.func_ea);
    if ( pfn == nullptr )
    {
      log.cat_sprnt("SKIP rename at %a: couldn't create/find a function there\n", r.func_ea);
      continue;
    }

    if ( set_name(r.func_ea, r.name.c_str(), SN_FORCE | SN_NOWARN) )
      ++renamed_count;

    if ( !r.comment.empty() && set_func_cmt(pfn, r.comment.c_str(), true) )
      ++commented_count;
  }

  qstring log_path = write_scan_log(finals, log);

  msg(
      "SM Lua Function Finder: found %d Lua-registered function(s) across %d "
      "distinct addresses, renamed %d, added %d doc comment(s). Log: %s\n",
      int(regs.size()),
      int(finals.size()),
      int(renamed_count),
      int(commented_count),
      log_path.empty() ? "<failed to write>" : log_path.c_str());

  scan_results_popup_t popup(finals);
  popup.choose();
  return true;
}

plugmod_t *idaapi init()
{
  if ( !init_hexrays_plugin() )
    return nullptr;
  if ( !install_hexrays_callback(decompiler_callback, nullptr) )
    msg("%s: failed to install the Hex-Rays callback\n", PLUGIN.wanted_name);
  return PLUGIN_OK;
}

void idaapi term()
{
  remove_hexrays_callback(decompiler_callback, nullptr);
}

} // namespace

plugin_t PLUGIN =
{
  IDP_INTERFACE_VERSION,
  0,
  init,
  term,
  run,
  "Discovers Lua-callable C functions registered via luaL_register() "
  "(sm.* namespaces and userdata methods) and renames them Lua_<Owner>_<func>, "
  "using the sm_api_differ json dump for doc comments where available.",
  "",
  "SM Lua Function Finder",
  ""
};

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/reg_scan.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "reg_scan.h"
#include "lua_reg_array.h"

#include <hexrays.hpp>

#include <name.hpp>
#include <xref.hpp>
#include <funcs.hpp>
#include <bytes.hpp>

#include <qset.hpp>

#include <algorithm>

namespace
{

const cexpr_t *skip_casts(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  while ( e != nullptr && e->op == cot_cast )
    e = e->x;
  return e;
}

bool is_call_to(const cexpr_t *e, ea_t target_ea)
{
  e = skip_casts(e);
  if ( e == nullptr || e->op != cot_call )
    return false;
  const cexpr_t *callee = skip_casts(e->x);
  return callee != nullptr && callee->op == cot_obj && callee->obj_ea == target_ea;
}

// Resolves a "const char *" argument to its string contents. Handles two
// shapes seen in practice:
//   - a literal string constant folded directly into the call
//   - a global pointer variable read by value (e.g. a cached "Tool" name
//     stashed in a static local next to a typeid-hash cache) -- the global
//     itself holds a pointer to the string, so it needs one dereference.
bool resolve_str_arg(const cexpr_t *e, qstring *out)
{
  e = skip_casts(e);
  if ( e == nullptr )
    return false;

  if ( e->op == cot_str )
  {
    if ( e->string == nullptr )
      return false;
    *out = e->string;
    return true;
  }

  if ( e->op == cot_obj )
  {
    ea_t g = e->obj_ea;
    if ( !is_loaded(g) )
      return false;
    ea_t p = ea_t(get_qword(g));
    if ( p == 0 || !is_loaded(p) )
      return false;
    return get_strlit_contents(out, p, -1, STRTYPE_C) > 0;
  }

  return false;
}

// Resolves the "const luaL_Reg *" argument to the ea of the array it points
// at. The compiler always takes this array's address explicitly (&global),
// which shows up in the ctree as cot_ref(cot_obj(obj_ea=array)).
bool resolve_array_ea(const cexpr_t *e, ea_t *arr_ea)
{
  e = skip_casts(e);
  if ( e == nullptr )
    return false;

  if ( e->op == cot_ref )
  {
    const cexpr_t *inner = skip_casts(e->x);
    if ( inner != nullptr && inner->op == cot_obj )
    {
      *arr_ea = inner->obj_ea;
      return true;
    }
    return false;
  }

  // Defensive fallback: some shapes may present the array address as a
  // plain object reference without an explicit cot_ref wrapper.
  if ( e->op == cot_obj )
  {
    *arr_ea = e->obj_ea;
    return true;
  }

  return false;
}

struct newmt_hit_t
{
  ea_t call_ea;
  qstring name;
};

struct register_hit_t
{
  ea_t call_ea;
  bool libname_is_null;
  qstring libname_str;
  bool arr_resolved;
  ea_t arr_ea;
};

struct reg_call_visitor_t final : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  ea_t register_ea;
  ea_t newmetatable_ea;
  qvector<newmt_hit_t> newmt_hits;
  qvector<register_hit_t> register_hits;

  reg_call_visitor_t(ea_t register_ea_, ea_t newmetatable_ea_)
    : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST), register_ea(register_ea_), newmetatable_ea(newmetatable_ea_)
  {
  }

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op != cot_call || e->a == nullptr )
      return 0;

    const cexpr_t *callee = skip_casts(e->x);
    if ( callee == nullptr || callee->op != cot_obj )
      return 0;

    if ( callee->obj_ea == newmetatable_ea && e->a->size() >= 2 )
    {
      newmt_hit_t hit;
      hit.call_ea = e->ea;
      if ( resolve_str_arg(&(*e->a)[1], &hit.name) )
        newmt_hits.push_back(hit);
      return 0;
    }

    if ( callee->obj_ea == register_ea && e->a->size() >= 3 )
    {
      register_hit_t hit;
      hit.call_ea = e->ea;
      const cexpr_t &libname_arg = (*e->a)[1];
      const cexpr_t *libname = skip_casts(&libname_arg);
      hit.libname_is_null = libname != nullptr && libname->op == cot_num && libname->is_zero_const();
      if ( !hit.libname_is_null )
      {
        if ( !resolve_str_arg(&libname_arg, &hit.libname_str) )
          return 0; // unresolvable libname shape -- skip this call site entirely
      }
      hit.arr_resolved = resolve_array_ea(&(*e->a)[2], &hit.arr_ea);
      register_hits.push_back(hit);
      return 0;
    }

    return 0;
  }
};

bool less_by_call_ea_newmt(const newmt_hit_t &a, const newmt_hit_t &b)
{
  return a.call_ea < b.call_ea;
}

bool less_by_call_ea_reg(const register_hit_t &a, const register_hit_t &b)
{
  return a.call_ea < b.call_ea;
}

qvector<ea_t> find_caller_func_starts(ea_t target_ea)
{
  qvector<ea_t> out;
  qset<ea_t> seen;
  xrefblk_t xb;
  for ( bool ok = xb.first_to(target_ea, XREF_ALL); ok; ok = xb.next_to() )
  {
    if ( xb.type != fl_CN && xb.type != fl_CF )
      continue;
    func_t *pfn = get_func(xb.from);
    if ( pfn == nullptr )
      continue;
    if ( seen.find(pfn->start_ea) == seen.end() )
    {
      seen.insert(pfn->start_ea);
      out.push_back(pfn->start_ea);
    }
  }
  return out;
}

void process_function(
    ea_t func_start,
    ea_t register_ea,
    ea_t newmetatable_ea,
    qvector<lua_func_reg_t> *out,
    qstring *log)
{
  func_t *pfn = get_func(func_start);
  if ( pfn == nullptr )
    return;

  hexrays_failure_t hf;
  cfuncptr_t cf = decompile_func(pfn, &hf);
  if ( cf == nullptr )
  {
    log->cat_sprnt("SKIP function %a: decompilation failed (%s)\n", func_start, hf.desc().c_str());
    return;
  }

  reg_call_visitor_t visitor(register_ea, newmetatable_ea);
  visitor.apply_to(&cf->body, nullptr);

  std::sort(visitor.newmt_hits.begin(), visitor.newmt_hits.end(), less_by_call_ea_newmt);
  std::sort(visitor.register_hits.begin(), visitor.register_hits.end(), less_by_call_ea_reg);

  for ( const register_hit_t &reg : visitor.register_hits )
  {
    if ( !reg.arr_resolved )
    {
      log->cat_sprnt(
          "SKIP call %a in %a: couldn't resolve the luaL_Reg array argument\n",
          reg.call_ea, func_start);
      continue;
    }

    qstring owner;
    bool is_userdata;
    if ( reg.libname_is_null )
    {
      is_userdata = true;
      const newmt_hit_t *preceding = nullptr;
      for ( const newmt_hit_t &nh : visitor.newmt_hits )
      {
        if ( nh.call_ea <= reg.call_ea )
          preceding = &nh;
        else
          break;
      }
      if ( preceding == nullptr )
      {
        log->cat_sprnt(
            "SKIP call %a in %a: NULL-libname luaL_register with no preceding "
            "luaL_newmetatable in this function\n",
            reg.call_ea, func_start);
        continue;
      }
      owner = preceding->name;
    }
    else
    {
      is_userdata = false;
      owner = reg.libname_str.empty() ? qstring("GLOBAL") : reg.libname_str;
    }

    qstring reason;
    qvector<lua_reg_entry_t> entries = parse_luaL_reg_array(reg.arr_ea, &reason);
    if ( entries.empty() )
    {
      log->cat_sprnt(
          "SKIP call %a in %a: luaL_Reg array at %a yielded no entries (%s)\n",
          reg.call_ea, func_start, reg.arr_ea,
          reason.empty() ? "empty array" : reason.c_str());
      continue;
    }

    for ( const lua_reg_entry_t &entry : entries )
    {
      if ( entry.func_ea == 0 || !is_loaded(entry.func_ea) )
        continue;

      lua_func_reg_t rec;
      rec.owner = owner;
      rec.is_userdata = is_userdata;
      rec.funcname = entry.name;
      rec.func_ea = entry.func_ea;
      rec.call_ea = reg.call_ea;
      out->push_back(rec);
    }
  }
}

} // namespace

qvector<lua_func_reg_t> scan_lua_registrations(qstring *log)
{
  qvector<lua_func_reg_t> out;

  ea_t register_ea = get_name_ea(BADADDR, "luaL_register");
  ea_t newmetatable_ea = get_name_ea(BADADDR, "luaL_newmetatable");
  if ( register_ea == BADADDR || newmetatable_ea == BADADDR )
  {
    log->cat_sprnt(
        "luaL_register/luaL_newmetatable not found by name -- is lua51.dll "
        "imported and named normally in this database?\n");
    return out;
  }

  qset<ea_t> func_set;
  for ( ea_t ea : find_caller_func_starts(register_ea) )
    func_set.insert(ea);
  for ( ea_t ea : find_caller_func_starts(newmetatable_ea) )
    func_set.insert(ea);

  for ( ea_t func_start : func_set )
    process_function(func_start, register_ea, newmetatable_ea, &out, log);

  return out;
}

```

`sm-lua-func-finder/src/reg_scan.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>

// One Lua-callable C function discovered through a luaL_register() call --
// either a static namespace function (owner = dotted path, e.g. "sm.audio")
// or a userdata method (owner = the luaL_newmetatable() type name that the
// enclosing luaL_register(L, NULL, ...) call registers into, e.g. "Character").
struct lua_func_reg_t
{
  qstring owner;
  bool is_userdata;
  qstring funcname;
  ea_t func_ea;
  ea_t call_ea; // address of the luaL_register() call site, for diagnostics
};

// Scans every function in the database that calls luaL_register or
// luaL_newmetatable, decompiles it, and walks its ctree to recover the
// (owner, funcname, func_ea) triples described above. Diagnostic lines
// (missing imports, undecodable call sites, etc.) are appended to *log.
qvector<lua_func_reg_t> scan_lua_registrations(qstring *log);

```

`stl-utility/README.md`:

```md
# IDA-STL-Utility

One-click retyping and content preview for MSVC STL containers in Hex-Rays
pseudocode. Built to solve the biggest recurring pain point when reversing
MSVC binaries: `std::string`'s small-string-optimization (SSO) union, which
Hex-Rays can't resolve on its own, so it never shows the real string content.

### NOTE: This project is fully AI generated, I don't take any credit for anything in here.

## Supported types (v1)

- `std::basic_string<char>` / `std::basic_string<wchar_t>` (`std::string` /
  `std::wstring`), including SSO-aware content decoding.
- `std::shared_ptr<T>` / `std::weak_ptr<T>`, with strong/weak refcount preview.
- `std::vector<T>`, with element-count/capacity preview.
- `std::unique_ptr<T>` (stateless-deleter/EBO case).
- `std::map` / `std::set` / `std::multimap` / `std::multiset`: **header-only
  stub** -- shows element count and the root/sentinel pointer; full node
  traversal is not implemented in v1.
- `std::unordered_map` / `std::unordered_set`: **header-only stub** -- shows
  element count, bucket count, and max load factor; full node traversal is
  not implemented in v1. Unlike this plugin's other descriptors, this
  layout is derived purely from the MSVC STL header source and has **not**
  been verified against a live/decompiled binary (this plugin's usual real-
  world test target has no `unordered_map`/`unordered_set` usage anywhere
  in it) -- treat the applied type with a bit more skepticism than the
  others.

Additionally, this plugin collapses several kinds of inlined MSVC STL
scope-exit teardown boilerplate directly in Hex-Rays pseudocode (not tied to
the retype action above -- these run automatically on every decompile):

- Inlined `std::shared_ptr`/`std::weak_ptr` release sequences, collapsed to
  `~shared_ptr(obj);`.
- Inlined `std::basic_string` (SSO-aware) release sequences, collapsed to
  `~string(obj);`.
- Inlined `std::function`/delegate-closure teardown sequences (the
  `std::_Func_impl_no_alloc<...>` internal storage behind `std::function`),
  collapsed to `~function(obj);`.
- Inlined `std::vector<T>::~vector()` for TRIVIALLY DESTRUCTIBLE element
  types `T` only (pointers, ints, PODs, structs without destructors -- the
  overwhelmingly common case), collapsed to `~vector(obj);`. For a trivial
  `T`, MSVC elides the per-element destroy loop entirely, so the whole
  destructor is just `if ( v->_Myfirst ) operator delete(v->_Myfirst);` (or
  the sized-delete form). The non-trivial-element case (a loop destroying
  each element before the free) is a different, harder shape and is **not**
  handled -- it's simply left uncollapsed, not mis-collapsed.

## Usage

Put the cursor on a variable, parameter, or global in Hex-Rays pseudocode
and press **Ctrl-Shift-T** (or right-click and choose **IDA-STL-Utility ->
Retype as STL container**).

- If exactly one supported type matches with high confidence (e.g. the
  variable's type name already contains `basic_string`, `shared_ptr<`,
  `vector<`, etc.), it's applied immediately.
- If the match is ambiguous (e.g. an opaque same-size buffer that could be
  more than one type), a numbered prompt lets you pick.
- Once retyped, a trailing comment renders automatically on every
  subsequent decompile of that function, e.g.:

  ```c
  std::string name;          // "hello world" (size=11 cap=15, inline)
  std::shared_ptr<Foo> ptr;  // strong=2 weak=1
  std::vector<int> values;   // size=3 cap=8 (elements, 4 bytes/element)
  ```

  (`std::unique_ptr<T>` only shows its preview comment immediately after the
  retype action runs, since a plain pointer can't be distinguished from any
  other pointer of the same type on later decompiles.)

## Content preview: static vs. live

- With a debugger attached, previews are read from live process memory,
  including heap-allocated content (SSO's "big string" arm, `shared_ptr`
  control blocks, `vector` backing storage).
- Without a debugger, previews are read from the static database image
  (`.rdata`/`.data`), which works well for fixed globals/statics with a real
  initializer, but **cannot** recover:
  - Stack-local temporaries (no fixed address without a live debugger at the
    right frame) -- shown as `(stack-local, attach debugger to preview)`.
  - Heap-allocated content with no debugger attached -- shown as `(heap,
    attach debugger to preview)`.

## Requirements

- IDA Professional 9.2 or newer
- Hex-Rays decompiler
- IDA SDK configured through the `IDASDK` environment variable (a checkout of
  [HexRaysSA/ida-sdk](https://github.com/HexRaysSA/ida-sdk) at a tag with
  CMake support, e.g. `v9.3.1-release` -- not the classic hex-rays SDK zip)
- CMake 3.27 or newer
- Visual Studio with C++ build tools on Windows

## Build

```powershell
$env:IDASDK = "C:\path\to\ida-sdk"
cmake -S . -B build -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64
cmake --build build --config Release
```

On Windows, the build installs the DLL and metadata into IDA's plugin
directory automatically if it has write access; otherwise copy
`stl_retype.dll` and `ida-plugin.json` into
`<IDA install>\plugins\stl_retype\` yourself (this typically needs an
elevated copy, since `Program Files` is admin-writable only).

## Diagnostic output

Diagnostic output is written to:

```text
%APPDATA%\Hex-Rays\IDA Pro\IDA-STL-Utility.log
```

## Known limitations (v1)

- x64 only (MSVC VS2019/2022 ABI). 32-bit targets are not supported.
- `std::map`/`std::set` only expose the tree header (count + root pointer),
  not full node/iterator traversal.
- `std::unordered_map`/`std::unordered_set` only expose the hash-table
  header (count, bucket count, max load factor), not full node/iterator
  traversal; and this layout is header-derived, not verified against a
  live/decompiled binary.
- `std::vector<T>`'s destructor/release collapse only handles trivially
  destructible element types `T`; a non-trivial per-element destructor loop
  is not recognized.
- `std::vector<T>`/`std::unique_ptr<T>` prompt for the element type `T` when
  it can't be recovered from existing type info.
- `std::unique_ptr<T>` with a stateful deleter (not the default
  `std::default_delete<T>`) uses a different layout than what this plugin
  assumes; not supported.
- Synthetic Local Types this plugin creates are named `__stl_*` to avoid
  colliding with any real PDB-derived STL type of the same name; if a valid
  real STL type already exists in Local Types (matching size/shape), it's
  reused instead and the real name shows up in pseudocode.

```

`stl-utility/src/stl_function_release.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "stl_function_release.h"

#include <typeinf.hpp>

#include <set>

namespace stl_utility
{

namespace
{

const cexpr_t *strip_casts(const cexpr_t *e)
{
  while ( e != nullptr && e->op == cot_cast )
    e = e->x;
  return e;
}

bool is_num32(const cexpr_t *e, uint32 value)
{
  return e != nullptr && e->op == cot_num && uint32(e->numval()) == value;
}

bool same_expr(const cexpr_t *a, const cexpr_t *b)
{
  a = strip_casts(a);
  b = strip_casts(b);
  if ( a == nullptr || b == nullptr )
    return false;
  return a->equal_effect(*b);
}

// True if `stmt` is a `cit_expr` statement whose (cast-stripped) expression
// is a `cot_call` through something other than a plain named function
// reference -- i.e. some indirect/computed call, such as the vtable-slot-4
// virtual dispatch (`v13->VTABLE[0x4](v7)`) this matcher is looking for.
// Deliberately does NOT validate the callee's exact vtable-index-4 shape
// (it can print several different ways depending on Hex-Rays version); the
// surrounding bare-pointer condition + trailing null-out give this enough
// specificity, mirroring how stl_shared_ptr_release.cpp's destroy_call and
// stl_string_release.cpp's free call also aren't deeply inspected.
bool is_indirect_call_stmt(const cinsn_t &stmt)
{
  if ( stmt.op != cit_expr || stmt.cexpr == nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *call = strip_casts(stmt.cexpr);
  if ( call == nullptr || call->op != cot_call )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *callee = strip_casts(call->x);
  return callee != nullptr && callee->op != cot_obj;
}

// True if `stmt` is `P = 0;` (tolerating cast wrapping on either side), i.e.
// the tracking pointer being nulled out after teardown.
bool is_null_out_stmt(const cinsn_t &stmt, const cexpr_t *ptr_expr)
{
  if ( stmt.op != cit_expr || stmt.cexpr == nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *asg = strip_casts(stmt.cexpr);
  if ( asg == nullptr || asg->op != cot_asg )
    return false;

  if ( !same_expr(asg->x, ptr_expr) )
    return false;

  return is_num32(strip_casts(asg->y), 0);
}

struct release_match_t
{
  cinsn_t *matched_stmt = nullptr;
  const cexpr_t *sp_expr = nullptr;
};

bool try_match_function_release(cinsn_t *outer_if, release_match_t *out)
{
  if ( outer_if == nullptr || outer_if->op != cit_if || outer_if->cif == nullptr )
    return false;
  if ( outer_if->cif->ielse != nullptr )
    return false;

  const cexpr_t *ptr_expr = strip_casts(&outer_if->cif->expr);
  if ( ptr_expr == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *body = outer_if->cif->ithen;
  if ( body == nullptr || body->op != cit_block || body->cblock == nullptr )
    return false;

  bool found_call = false;
  bool found_null_out = false;
  for ( cinsn_t &stmt : *body->cblock )
  {
    if ( !found_call )
    {
      if ( is_indirect_call_stmt(stmt) )
        found_call = true;
      continue;
    }

    // Already saw the indirect call; now look for the trailing null-out.
    if ( is_null_out_stmt(stmt, ptr_expr) )
    {
      found_null_out = true;
      break;
    }
  }

  if ( !found_call || !found_null_out )
    return false;

  out->matched_stmt = outer_if;
  out->sp_expr = ptr_expr;
  return true;
}

bool synthesize_and_replace(const release_match_t &match)
{
  if ( match.matched_stmt == nullptr || match.sp_expr == nullptr )
    return false;

  tinfo_t void_type;
  if ( !void_type.create_simple_type(BTF_VOID) )
    return false;

  carglist_t *args = new carglist_t();
  args->resize(1);
  args->at(0).consume_cexpr(new cexpr_t(*match.sp_expr));

  // Render as a destructor-call-shaped helper, e.g. "~function(v13)", rather
  // than a generic "release(v13)". Hex-Rays' call_helper()/vcall_helper()
  // (see hexrays.hpp) only ever print a helper expression as "name(args...)"
  // -- the name is an arbitrary string the decompiler prints verbatim, not a
  // real function, and there is no ctree construct available to us that
  // prints true member-destructor syntax ("obj.~Type()"); that form only
  // appears when Hex-Rays itself recognizes an actual destructor call, which
  // is not what we're synthesizing here. So "~function(...)" is the closest
  // achievable approximation, and importantly the object argument below is
  // still a real cloned cexpr_t (not text baked into the helper name), so it
  // stays clickable/renameable in the pseudocode.
  cexpr_t *call = call_helper(void_type, args, "~function");
  if ( call == nullptr )
    return false;

  cinsn_t *new_stmt = new cinsn_t();
  new_stmt->op = cit_expr;
  new_stmt->cexpr = call;
  new_stmt->ea = match.matched_stmt->ea;

  match.matched_stmt->replace_by(new_stmt);
  return true;
}

struct function_release_collapser_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  int collapsed_count = 0;

  function_release_collapser_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  int idaapi visit_insn(cinsn_t *insn) override
  {
    if ( insn->op != cit_if )
      return 0;

    release_match_t match;
    if ( !try_match_function_release(insn, &match) )
      return 0;
    if ( synthesize_and_replace(match) )
      ++collapsed_count;
    return 0;
  }
};

struct used_var_collector_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  std::set<int> used_indices;

  used_var_collector_t() : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST) {}

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op == cot_var )
      used_indices.insert(e->v.idx);
    return 0;
  }
};

// See stl_shared_ptr_release.cpp for the rationale: replace_by deletes
// statements but leaves now-orphaned locals (the cached storage/tracking
// pointer, e.g. "v13") declared-but-unused, since their CVAR_USED flag isn't
// recalculated after our mutation. Hide anything no longer referenced,
// skipping arguments (never hide those -- it would alter the printed
// function signature, not just a declaration line).
void hide_orphaned_locals(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  used_var_collector_t collector;
  collector.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);

  lvars_t *lvars = cfunc->get_lvars();
  if ( lvars == nullptr )
    return;

  for ( size_t i = 0; i < lvars->size(); ++i )
  {
    lvar_t &lvar = lvars->at(i);
    if ( lvar.is_arg_var() || !lvar.used() )
      continue;
    if ( collector.used_indices.find(int(i)) == collector.used_indices.end() )
      lvar.clear_used();
  }
}

} // namespace

int collapse_function_releases(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  function_release_collapser_t collapser;
  collapser.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);
  if ( collapser.collapsed_count > 0 )
    hide_orphaned_locals(cfunc);
  return collapser.collapsed_count;
}

} // namespace stl_utility

```

`stl-utility/src/stl_function_release.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <hexrays.hpp>

namespace stl_utility
{

// Recognizes the inlined MSVC std::_Func_impl_no_alloc<Callable,...> teardown
// sequence -- the internal type behind std::function/delegate-style callback
// storage. A std::function is constructed on the stack (self-pointer "v13" =
// "v12", the SBO-indicator tracking pointer), passed by pointer to some
// registration/dispatch call, then torn down inline with this exact shape:
//
//   if ( v13 )
//   {
//     v7 = v12;                    // copy of the base/storage pointer
//     LOBYTE(v7) = v13 != v12;     // "is heap-allocated" flag, repurposing v7's low byte
//     v13->VTABLE[0x4](v7);        // std::_Func_class::_Delete_this(bool), through the object's own vtable
//     v13 = nullptr;
//   }
//
// This is pure ABI boilerplate emitted at every inlined std::function scope
// exit -- not useful to a reverse engineer -- so it's collapsed into a
// single `~function(v13);`-shaped synthetic call, mirroring
// stl_shared_ptr_release.cpp's approach for shared_ptr/weak_ptr release.
// Runs at hxe_maturity/CMAT_FINAL, mutating the ctree via
// cinsn_t::replace_by before pseudocode text is generated.
//
// The flag-computation setup statement(s) ("v7 = v12", "LOBYTE(v7) = ...")
// are decompiler-representation-sensitive and are NOT required by the
// matcher; the strict, distinctive backstop is the bare-pointer `if`
// condition plus an indirect/computed call somewhere in the block followed
// later by the tracking pointer being nulled out -- mirroring how the
// string-release matcher leans on a distinctive member-access shape rather
// than validating callee/vtable-slot names precisely.
//
// Returns the number of collapsed occurrences within `cfunc`'s body.
int collapse_function_releases(cfunc_t *cfunc);

} // namespace stl_utility

```

`stl-utility/src/stl_memory.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "stl_memory.h"

#include <bytes.hpp>
#include <dbg.hpp>
#include <segment.hpp>

#include <cctype>
#include <string>
#include <utility>

namespace stl_utility
{

stl_read_result_t stl_read_memory(ea_t ea, size_t size)
{
  if ( ea == BADADDR || size == 0 )
    return {};

  if ( is_debugger_on() )
  {
    bytevec_t buf;
    buf.resize(size);
    const ssize_t got = read_dbg_memory(ea, buf.begin(), size);
    if ( got == ssize_t(size) )
    {
      stl_read_result_t result;
      result.ok = true;
      result.from_debugger = true;
      result.data = std::move(buf);
      return result;
    }
  }

  bytevec_t buf;
  buf.resize(size);
  if ( get_bytes(buf.begin(), ssize_t(size), ea) == ssize_t(size) )
  {
    stl_read_result_t result;
    result.ok = true;
    result.from_debugger = false;
    result.data = std::move(buf);
    return result;
  }
  return {};
}

bool looks_like_stack_address(ea_t ea)
{
  if ( ea == BADADDR )
    return false;

  segment_t *seg = getseg(ea);
  if ( seg == nullptr )
  {
    // No fixed segment at all (e.g. a live-only heap/stack address that
    // only exists once a debugger session created it): treat as non-static.
    return true;
  }

  qstring name;
  get_segm_name(&name, seg);
  std::string lowered(name.c_str());
  for ( char &c : lowered )
    c = char(std::tolower(static_cast<unsigned char>(c)));
  return lowered.find("stack") != std::string::npos;
}

} // namespace stl_utility

```

`stl-utility/src/stl_memory.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <pro.h>
#include <ida.hpp>

namespace stl_utility
{

struct stl_read_result_t
{
  bool ok = false;
  bool from_debugger = false;
  bytevec_t data;
};

// Reads `size` bytes at `ea`: from the live debuggee if a debugger session
// is attached (falling back to static bytes if the live read fails, e.g. the
// page isn't currently mapped), otherwise from the static database image
// (.rdata/.data initializer bytes).
stl_read_result_t stl_read_memory(ea_t ea, size_t size);

// True if `ea` looks like a stack address rather than a fixed global/static
// address -- i.e. content there is only meaningful with a live debugger
// positioned at the right frame, and a static read would return garbage or
// merely the image's uninitialized stack-segment bytes.
bool looks_like_stack_address(ea_t ea);

} // namespace stl_utility

`stl-utility/src/stl_registry.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "stl_registry.h"

namespace stl_utility
{

void register_stl_string_descriptors(std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> *out);
void register_stl_shared_ptr_descriptors(std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> *out);
void register_stl_vector_descriptors(std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> *out);
void register_stl_unique_ptr_descriptors(std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> *out);
void register_stl_tree_descriptors(std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> *out);
void register_stl_unordered_map_descriptors(std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> *out);

namespace
{

std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> build_registry()
{
  std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> result;
  register_stl_string_descriptors(&result);
  register_stl_shared_ptr_descriptors(&result);
  register_stl_vector_descriptors(&result);
  register_stl_unique_ptr_descriptors(&result);
  register_stl_tree_descriptors(&result);
  register_stl_unordered_map_descriptors(&result);
  return result;
}

} // namespace

const std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> &get_stl_descriptor_registry()
{
  static const std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> registry = build_registry();
  return registry;
}

} // namespace stl_utility

`stl-utility/src/stl_registry.h`:

```h
#pragma once

#include <hexrays.hpp>

#include <vector>

namespace stl_utility
{

enum match_confidence_t
{
  MATCH_NONE = 0,
  MATCH_AMBIGUOUS,
  MATCH_HIGH,
};

struct stl_decode_result_t
{
  bool ok = false;
  qstring preview;
  bool from_live_memory = false;
};

// One implementation per supported MSVC STL container. See
// C:\Users\Ben\.claude\plans\spicy-imagining-church.md for the design.
struct stl_type_descriptor_t
{
  const char *key;           // stable internal id, e.g. "std_string_char"
  const char *display_name;  // shown in the "pick a type" chooser

  // Decide if this descriptor plausibly matches a variable/global's current
  // (possibly empty/void) type and/or a hint string (its current type's
  // printable name, or an empty string if unknown).
  match_confidence_t (*matches)(
        const tinfo_t *existing_type,
        const qstring *demangled_name_hint);

  // Resolve the tinfo_t to apply. `existing_type` (possibly empty) and
  // `demangled_name_hint` (possibly empty) are the same context passed to
  // `matches()`, so descriptors that need a template argument (e.g.
  // std::vector<T>'s element type) can try to recover it from the existing
  // type/name before falling back to an interactive prompt.
  //
  // Implementations should first try tinfo_t::get_named_type() for the
  // exact real STL name and validate its size/shape before reusing it; only
  // build+register a plugin-owned synthetic type as a fallback. Returns
  // false if a type couldn't be resolved (e.g. the user cancelled a
  // required prompt, such as vector's element-type prompt).
  bool (*build_or_get_type)(
        til_t *til,
        const tinfo_t *existing_type,
        const qstring *demangled_name_hint,
        tinfo_t *out_type,
        qstring *out_name);

  // Decode a human-readable content preview for an instance at
  // `instance_ea` (BADADDR if the instance has no fixed address, e.g. an
  // unresolved stack local).
  stl_decode_result_t (*decode_preview)(
        ea_t instance_ea,
        const tinfo_t &resolved_type);
};

const std::vector<const stl_type_descriptor_t *> &get_stl_descriptor_registry();

} // namespace stl_utility

```

`stl-utility/src/stl_retype.cpp`:

```cpp
// IDA-STL-Utility: one-click MSVC STL container retyping and content
// preview for Hex-Rays pseudocode. Mirrors the build/plugin conventions of
// the sister IDA-VTable-Utility plugin. See
// C:\Users\Ben\.claude\plans\spicy-imagining-church.md for the design.

#include "stl_registry.h"
#include "stl_memory.h"
#include "stl_shared_ptr_release.h"
#include "stl_string_release.h"
#include "stl_function_release.h"
#include "stl_vector_release.h"
#include "stl_util.h"

#include <hexrays.hpp>
#include <kernwin.hpp>
#include <name.hpp>
#include <typeinf.hpp>

#include <map>
#include <vector>

namespace
{

using namespace stl_utility;

constexpr char ACTION_NAME[] = "stl_retype:retype";
constexpr char ACTION_LABEL[] = "Retype as STL container";
constexpr char ACTION_HOTKEY[] = "Ctrl-Shift-T";
constexpr char POPUP_PATH[] = "IDA-STL-Utility/";

void append_colored(qstring *output, const char *text, const char *color)
{
  output->append(SCOLOR_ON);
  output->append(color);
  output->append(text);
  output->append(SCOLOR_OFF);
  output->append(color);
}

// Resolves what's under the cursor to either an lvar or a global address,
// plus its current (possibly empty/void) type.
struct retype_target_t
{
  lvar_t *lvar = nullptr;
  ea_t ea = BADADDR;
  tinfo_t existing_type;
};

bool resolve_target(vdui_t *vu, retype_target_t *out)
{
  if ( vu == nullptr || vu->item.citype != VDI_EXPR || vu->item.e == nullptr )
    return false;

  cexpr_t *e = vu->item.e;
  if ( e->op == cot_var )
  {
    out->lvar = &e->v.getv();
    out->ea = BADADDR;
    out->existing_type = out->lvar->type();
    return true;
  }
  if ( e->op == cot_obj )
  {
    out->lvar = nullptr;
    out->ea = e->obj_ea;
    get_tinfo(&out->existing_type, out->ea);
    return true;
  }
  return false;
}

struct candidate_t
{
  const stl_type_descriptor_t *descriptor;
  match_confidence_t confidence;
};

std::vector<candidate_t> collect_candidates(const tinfo_t &existing_type, const qstring &hint)
{
  std::vector<candidate_t> candidates;
  const tinfo_t *type_ptr = existing_type.empty() ? nullptr : &existing_type;
  const qstring *hint_ptr = hint.empty() ? nullptr : &hint;
  for ( const stl_type_descriptor_t *descriptor : get_stl_descriptor_registry() )
  {
    const match_confidence_t confidence = descriptor->matches(type_ptr, hint_ptr);
    if ( confidence != MATCH_NONE )
      candidates.push_back({ descriptor, confidence });
  }
  return candidates;
}

// Picks one descriptor among several candidates via a simple numbered
// prompt (ask_long), since a single high-confidence match wasn't found.
const stl_type_descriptor_t *choose_candidate(const std::vector<candidate_t> &candidates)
{
  if ( candidates.size() == 1 )
    return candidates[0].descriptor;

  qstring prompt = "Multiple/ambiguous STL types could apply. Pick one:\n";
  for ( size_t i = 0; i < candidates.size(); ++i )
    prompt.cat_sprnt("%d) %s\n", int(i + 1), candidates[i].descriptor->display_name);

  sval_t choice = 1;
  if ( !ask_long(&choice, "%s", prompt.c_str()) )
    return nullptr;
  if ( choice < 1 || size_t(choice) > candidates.size() )
  {
    warning("IDA-STL-Utility: invalid selection");
    return nullptr;
  }
  return candidates[size_t(choice) - 1].descriptor;
}

bool perform_retype(vdui_t *vu)
{
  retype_target_t target;
  if ( !resolve_target(vu, &target) )
  {
    warning("IDA-STL-Utility: place the cursor on a variable or global to retype");
    return false;
  }

  const qstring hint = printable_type_name(target.existing_type);
  const std::vector<candidate_t> candidates = collect_candidates(target.existing_type, hint);
  if ( candidates.empty() )
  {
    warning("IDA-STL-Utility: no supported STL type recognized for this item");
    return false;
  }

  size_t high_count = 0;
  for ( const candidate_t &c : candidates )
    high_count += (c.confidence == MATCH_HIGH) ? 1 : 0;

  const stl_type_descriptor_t *chosen = nullptr;
  if ( high_count == 1 )
  {
    for ( const candidate_t &c : candidates )
    {
      if ( c.confidence == MATCH_HIGH )
      {
        chosen = c.descriptor;
        break;
      }
    }
  }
  else
  {
    chosen = choose_candidate(candidates);
  }

  if ( chosen == nullptr )
    return false;

  tinfo_t resolved_type;
  qstring resolved_name;
  const tinfo_t *type_ptr = target.existing_type.empty() ? nullptr : &target.existing_type;
  const qstring *hint_ptr = hint.empty() ? nullptr : &hint;
  if ( !chosen->build_or_get_type(nullptr, type_ptr, hint_ptr, &resolved_type, &resolved_name) )
  {
    warning("IDA-STL-Utility: could not resolve the %s type", chosen->display_name);
    return false;
  }

  bool applied = false;
  if ( target.lvar != nullptr )
    applied = vu->set_lvar_type(target.lvar, resolved_type);
  else
    applied = apply_tinfo(target.ea, resolved_type, TINFO_DEFINITE);

  if ( !applied )
  {
    warning("IDA-STL-Utility: failed to apply type %s", resolved_name.c_str());
    return false;
  }

  debug_log(
    "retype: descriptor=%s target=%s applied_type=%s",
    chosen->key,
    target.lvar != nullptr ? target.lvar->name.c_str() : "<global>",
    resolved_name.c_str());

  vu->refresh_view(true);
  return true;
}

struct retype_action_t final : action_handler_t
{
  int idaapi activate(action_activation_ctx_t *ctx) override
  {
    if ( ctx == nullptr || ctx->widget_type != BWN_PSEUDOCODE )
      return 0;
    return perform_retype(get_widget_vdui(ctx->widget)) ? 1 : 0;
  }

  action_state_t idaapi update(action_update_ctx_t *ctx) override
  {
    return ctx != nullptr && ctx->widget_type == BWN_PSEUDOCODE
         ? AST_ENABLE_FOR_WIDGET
         : AST_DISABLE_FOR_WIDGET;
  }
};

retype_action_t g_retype_action;

// Appends a rendered content-preview comment to every printed line whose
// underlying variable/global is currently typed as one of our recognized
// STL containers (MATCH_HIGH only -- ambiguous/ill-defined matches are not
// auto-rendered to avoid noisy false positives on unrelated same-size
// structs). Runs once per hxe_func_printed pass.
struct preview_visitor_t : public ctree_visitor_t
{
  cfunc_t *cfunc;
  std::map<int, qstring> line_comments;

  explicit preview_visitor_t(cfunc_t *f) : ctree_visitor_t(CV_FAST), cfunc(f) {}

  int idaapi visit_expr(cexpr_t *e) override
  {
    if ( e->op != cot_var && e->op != cot_obj )
      return 0;

    tinfo_t type;
    ea_t instance_ea = BADADDR;
    if ( e->op == cot_var )
    {
      type = e->v.getv().type();
    }
    else
    {
      instance_ea = e->obj_ea;
      get_tinfo(&type, instance_ea);
    }

    if ( type.empty() )
      return 0;

    const qstring hint = printable_type_name(type);
    for ( const stl_type_descriptor_t *descriptor : get_stl_descriptor_registry() )
    {
      if ( descriptor->matches(&type, &hint) != MATCH_HIGH )
        continue;

      const stl_decode_result_t decoded = descriptor->decode_preview(instance_ea, type);
      if ( !decoded.ok || decoded.preview.empty() )
        break;

      int x = 0, y = 0;
      if ( !cfunc->find_item_coords(e, &x, &y) || y < 0 || y >= cfunc->sv.size() )
        break;

      qstring &slot = line_comments[y];
      if ( !slot.empty() )
        slot.append("; ");
      slot.append(decoded.preview);
      break;
    }
    return 0;
  }
};

void render_content_previews(cfunc_t *cfunc)
{
  preview_visitor_t visitor(cfunc);
  visitor.apply_to(&cfunc->body, nullptr);

  for ( const auto &entry : visitor.line_comments )
  {
    simpleline_t &line = cfunc->sv[entry.first];
    append_colored(&line.line, "  // ", SCOLOR_AUTOCMT);
    append_colored(&line.line, entry.second.c_str(), SCOLOR_AUTOCMT);
  }
}

ssize_t idaapi decompiler_callback(void *, hexrays_event_t event, va_list va)
{
  if ( event == hxe_maturity )
  {
    cfunc_t *cfunc = va_arg(va, cfunc_t *);
    const ctree_maturity_t new_maturity = ctree_maturity_t(va_arg(va, int));
    if ( new_maturity == CMAT_FINAL )
    {
      const int collapsed = collapse_shared_ptr_releases(cfunc);
      if ( collapsed > 0 )
        debug_log("collapsed %d shared_ptr release sequence(s) in function=%a", collapsed, cfunc->entry_ea);

      const int collapsed_strings = collapse_string_releases(cfunc);
      if ( collapsed_strings > 0 )
        debug_log("collapsed %d string release sequence(s) in function=%a", collapsed_strings, cfunc->entry_ea);

      const int collapsed_functions = collapse_function_releases(cfunc);
      if ( collapsed_functions > 0 )
        debug_log("collapsed %d function release sequence(s) in function=%a", collapsed_functions, cfunc->entry_ea);

      const int collapsed_vectors = collapse_vector_releases(cfunc);
      if ( collapsed_vectors > 0 )
        debug_log("collapsed %d vector release sequence(s) in function=%a", collapsed_vectors, cfunc->entry_ea);
    }
    return 0;
  }

  if ( event == hxe_func_printed )
  {
    render_content_previews(va_arg(va, cfunc_t *));
    return 0;
  }

  if ( event == hxe_populating_popup )
  {
    TWidget *widget = va_arg(va, TWidget *);
    TPopupMenu *popup = va_arg(va, TPopupMenu *);
    va_arg(va, vdui_t *); // vu, unused: the action is always offered in pseudocode
    attach_action_to_popup(widget, popup, ACTION_NAME, POPUP_PATH);
    return 0;
  }

  return 0;
}

struct stl_retype_plugmod_t final : plugmod_t
{
  stl_retype_plugmod_t()
  {
    const action_desc_t desc = ACTION_DESC_LITERAL_PLUGMOD(
      ACTION_NAME,
      ACTION_LABEL,
      &g_retype_action,
      this,
      ACTION_HOTKEY,
      "Detect and apply the correct MSVC STL container type, with SSO-aware content preview",
      -1);

    if ( !register_action(desc) )
      msg("%s: failed to register action %s\n", PLUGIN.wanted_name, ACTION_NAME);

    if ( !install_hexrays_callback(decompiler_callback, this) )
      msg("%s: failed to install the Hex-Rays callback\n", PLUGIN.wanted_name);

    debug_log("plugin initialized: version=0.1.0");
  }

  ~stl_retype_plugmod_t() override
  {
    remove_hexrays_callback(decompiler_callback, this);
  }

  bool idaapi run(size_t) override
  {
    return false;
  }
};

plugmod_t *idaapi init()
{
  if ( !init_hexrays_plugin() )
    return nullptr;
  return new stl_retype_plugmod_t;
}

} // namespace

plugin_t PLUGIN =
{
  IDP_INTERFACE_VERSION,
  PLUGIN_HIDE | PLUGIN_MULTI,
  init,
  nullptr,
  nullptr,
  "One-click retyping and SSO-aware content preview for MSVC STL containers.",
  "",
  "IDA-STL-Utility",
  ""
};
